## Task details

Full task specifications, keyed by row id.

### HIST01

History site icons never show — user report: history entries show only the generic icon. The wiring LOOKS done (webpage.cpp:355 iconChanged -> HistoryManager::setIcon for non-OTR pages, HistoryManager::icon falls back to :graphics/defaulticon.png), so find the real break: suspects (a) m_icons is an in-memory QHash with no persistence — after restart EVERY entry is generic; (b) QWebEnginePage::iconChanged may not deliver favicons in Qt 6.12 without the page actually settling / may deliver empty icon; (c) icon lookup keyed by full URL while entries are keyed differently. Fix: persist favicons keyed by host (a small icon store like the old WebKit IconDatabase — files under the data dir, e.g. icons/<host>.png, loaded lazily), backfill history model icons for existing entries, verify iconChanged actually fires on Qt 6.12 (if not, fetch iconUrl via the profile's cookie-aware path or the favicon manager). Verify: visit a site -> history shows its favicon; RESTART -> icon persists; history menu + history dialog + completer all show real icons; make check PASS.

### MENU02

File-menu order — user request: 'New Tor Window' currently sits at the bottom of File menu (browsermainwindow.cpp ~line 579, after the Private Browsing checkbox). ### PTAB01

New Private Tab — user request: REPLACE the app-global 'Private Browsing' checkbox action with a 'New Private Tab' menu item placed directly under 'New Tab' in the File menu. Design: private browsing today is window-scoped (BrowserApplication::isPrivate -> OTR profile per window). A private TAB means a WebView on the off-the-record profile inside a normal window: newTab() currently hardcodes BrowserApplication::webEngineProfile() — add a private-tab path constructing WebView(QWebEngineProfile::offTheRecord or the app's OTR profile). Requirements: (a) OTR page in the shared window (history/cookie writes already gated on profile()->isOffTheRecord — verify each site that checks it: history add, icon store, autofill, cookie jar, disk cache — must all exclude the OTR page); (b) visual marker on the tab (e.g. 'Private' badge/mask icon + the existing private.png indicator in the location bar must reflect per-TAB state, not window-global); (c) the 'Private Browsing' checkbox in File menu is removed/replaced (window-global toggle stays internally for full-private windows but the user-facing entry becomes New Private Tab); (d) keyboard shortcut (Ctrl+Shift+P or similar) consistent with other browsers. Verify: private tab leaves no history entry/cookie after close while sibling normal tabs still record; marker visible; menu order New Tab -> New Private Tab; make check PASS.

### PREFS01

Preferences as a tab, not a dialog — user request: Preferences (Edit->Preferences) currently opens the modal SettingsDialog (browsermainwindow.cpp:1272 -> exec()). Modern browsers open settings in a tab. Implement: (a) host the SettingsDialog's content widget inside a tab (embed the dialog as a non-modal widget in a WebViewWithSearch slot or a plain widget tab — the dialog is Qt Widgets .ui so embedding the widget, not the dialog shell, is the direct path; a toolbar-less tab or an arora://preferences page wrapping it — pick whichever preserves all tabs of the settings .ui); (b) single-instance — opening Preferences when a settings tab exists focuses that tab; (c) changes still apply/persist exactly as today (the dialog's save-on-accept semantics need a non-modal equivalent — Apply-on-change or explicit Save button per current behavior); (d) closing the tab == closing the dialog (cancel path must still discard per current semantics). Verify: Preferences opens as a tab, no modal block, second open focuses the same tab, a setting changed in-tab persists across restart, make check PASS.

### TOR03

Tor window: remove the dedicated search box — user report: in a Tor window the nav-bar search widget shows as a small collapsed sliver (button mode, the showSearchBox default-off path) and they want it gone entirely. `BrowserMainWindow::setupNavigationBar` always constructs `m_toolbarSearch` into `m_navigationSplitter` (browsermainwindow.cpp ~1128) next to `locationBarStack()`. Gate it on `!BrowserApplication::isTorMode()`: in tor mode the widget must be fully hidden and the location bar take the whole splitter width — Tor windows keep omnibox search via the location bar (SRCH01 covers the same job; a separate engine box in a Tor window is also a privacy wart — divergent-engine leakage + wasted width). Mind the dereference sites before choosing hide-vs-skip-construction: `applySearchBoxVisibility()` (~1160), the Ctrl+K/focus path (~1556), `toolbarSearch()` accessor (~1592), and browserapplication.cpp:737 which qobject_casts toolbar widgets into ToolbarSearch for search-history sync — prefer hide-only (`setVisible(false)` + splitter sizing) unless audit shows all uses null-safe; skipping construction is also acceptable if every site is guarded. Ctrl+K in a tor window should focus the location bar or no-op, never crash on a hidden/absent widget. Non-tor windows: byte-identical behavior (button mode + opt-in field both still work). Verify: `--tor-window-smoke` PASS; tor window has no search widget (widget-tree dump or offscreen check); normal window unchanged; make check PASS.

### TOR04

Tor circuit/hops on the status bar — user request: a Tor window's bottom status bar should show how the traffic is connected — the circuit hops (fallback: surface it in the site shield panel instead). Feasible via existing plumbing: `TorManager` owns a `TorControl` client on the daemon's ControlPort (tor/tormanager.h, tor/torcontrol.h) — `getInfo("circuit-status")` returns `250+circuit-status=` lines `<CircID> <Status> <$FP~Nick,$FP~Nick,...> ...` where the Path column is the hop chain (guard -> middle -> exit); `getInfo("stream-status")` maps `StreamID -> CircID + Target` so the display can prefer the circuit carrying the current tab's streams (fallback: newest BUILT PURPOSE=GENERAL circuit); `setEvents({"CIRC"})` or a poll-on-tab-change drives live updates. Spec: (a) TorManager grows a circuit surface — e.g. `requestCircuitInfo()` + `circuitsChanged` signal emitting a parsed list of {circId, status, hops:[{fingerprint, nickname}]}; TorControl is already single-connection serialized so queries queue fine; do NOT open a second control connection. Optional per-hop country suffix via `ns/id/<fp>` -> `ip-to-country/<ip>` ONLY if cheap — no consensus/ns/all download. (b) BrowserMainWindow: tor-mode-only permanent status-bar widget via `statusBar()->addPermanentWidget` (same pattern as m_loadingIndicator/m_zoomControl ~line 237) — compact chain text like `Tor: <guard> -> <middle> -> <exit>`; states: `Tor: connecting...` while Bootstrapping, `Tor: no circuit` pre-stream, error text on Failed; updates on circuitsChanged + tab change. Keep it a plain QLabel — status-bar real estate is limited; tooltip or SitePanel row can carry fingerprints/countries. (c) FALLBACK per user: if the status-bar surface proves infeasible (e.g. control connection can't serve circuit queries), put a read-only 'Tor circuit' row inside SitePanel's tor section instead (src/locationbar/sitepanel.cpp) — status bar is the target, shield only if blocked. (d) no widget/row in non-tor windows. Verify: `--tor-window-smoke` PASS; circuit chain rendered from a real daemon or a stubbed circuit-status fixture (unit-test the parser on captured 250+ reply text); updates on CIRC events/tab switch; nothing added in normal windows; make check PASS.

### ICONS02

Preferences dialog icons uneven — user report: the settings sidebar icons are not aligned, some render smaller some bigger. Cause is identifiable: the UIP03 Vivaldi-style `pagesList` QListWidget (settings.ui ~line 30, icons set in settings.cpp ~128-144 via `AroraIcon::get` -> `QIcon::fromTheme`) never sets `iconSize`, so every item paints at whatever size the active theme yields for that name. The pageIcons names (go-home, edit-find, zoom-original, security-high, tab-new, go-next, user-bookmarks, system-run, list-add) resolve at different nominal sizes across themes — worst under the "Native" theme where the system set has mixed 16/22/24/32 assets, and when a name is missing and falls back to arora-legacy (Size=16) it lands at yet another size -> uneven icon column + label baseline drift. Fix: (a) set a fixed `pagesList->setIconSize(...)` — size should follow the style's PM_SmallIconSize or a deliberate constant (16 or 20/22 — pick what the bundled themes look right at, keeping the sidebar compact), plus `setUniformItemSizes(true)`; mind HiDPI (setIconSize is in device-independent px — correct) and re-apply if theme switching at runtime (ICONS01 combo calls AroraIcon::setTheme — verify icons re-render at the fixed size, not re-resolve bigger). (b) Sweep the rest of the dialog for the same miss: the iconThemeCombo preview rows (settings.cpp ~152-158 — each theme's own view-refresh glyph will differ in canvas size too) and any other icon-bearing widget in settings.ui — normalize there too if visible. (c) Do NOT patch individual icons in the icon themes themselves — normalize at the widget/iconSize level so every current and future theme behaves. Verify: open Preferences offscreen under each bundled theme (adwaita, breeze, tabler, native) — all sidebar icons share one pixel height and the text baselines align; combo previews uniform; make check PASS.

### DOWN01

Rich download cards (Opera/Vivaldi-style) — user request with reference screenshot: download items should expand into a detail card showing filename, source URL, destination path, Started/Finished timestamps, total size, current speed, a speed-over-time area graph, and 'Restart' + 'Show in File Manager' buttons. Today DownloadItem (downloaditem.ui) is a compact row: fileIcon + squeezed filename + QProgressBar + info label ('X of Y — Z/s, ~Ns remaining') + stop/tryAgain/open buttons; DownloadManager (downloads.ui) is a dialog listing them via DownloadModel. What exists: m_url captured (downloadmanager.cpp ~149), m_downloadTime QElapsedTimer, currentSpeed() (~589 — CUMULATIVE average bytesReceived/elapsed, unsuitable for the graph), 200ms update throttle (m_lastProgressTime), DownloadManager::dataString/timeString formatters. Spec: (a) SPEED SERIES — add a bounded ring buffer of instantaneous samples: on each receivedBytesChanged, push (elapsed ms, deltaBytes/deltaSeconds); ~2 samples/sec, keep last N (~120 = 60s window); on stateChanged->Completed/Interrupted freeze the series and store the finish QDateTime + final average. (b) CARD UI — expandable per item (collapsed = today's compact row; expand on click or a chevron like Vivaldi — an always-expanded variant is acceptable if markedly simpler, expansion preferred): SqueezeLabels for source URL + destination path (file:// elided middle), 'Started: <time>' + 'Finished: <time>' lines, total size, live speed text. (c) GRAPH — small custom QWidget (paintEvent, ~48px tall, follows the UIP04 statusbar-widget convention): filled area sparkline of the speed series, palette Text/Highlight colors (dark-scheme safe per UIP01), auto-scaled Y to peak sample, flat-line at zero when no samples; repaint on new samples only. (d) BUTTONS — 'Show in File Manager' reveals the finished file's folder (QDesktopServices::openUrl(QUrl::fromLocalFile(dir)) — Linux path, xdg-open; selecting the file itself is bonus); 'Restart' generalizes the existing tryAgain path to also appear on completed items (re-issue m_url through the normal download flow); keep stop/open as-is. (e) persisted/cleared downloads handled sanely: items restored after restart show what data is known (URL, filename, size, timestamps) and simply omit the graph. Verify: download a file — live card shows rising/falling graph + speed text updating ~2x/s, Finished timestamp on completion; Restart re-downloads; Show in File Manager opens the folder; collapsed row unchanged; make check PASS.

### SBAR01

(b) NET WIDGET: 'NetIndicator' — poll every ~1s the io counters (/proc/<pid>/io read_bytes/write_bytes) of the app's QtWebEngineProcess children — primarily the network-service utility process (identify via /proc/<pid>/cmdline — it carries --type=utility with a network service subtype marker; if not identifiable, sum all QtWebEngineProcess children and accept the cache-I/O noise — document the approximation honestly in a comment + tooltip) -> delta/time = current up/down rates -> '↓ 1.2 MB/s ↑ 84 kB/s'; DISPLAY RULE per user: visible only while nonzero/recently-active — hide (or collapse to zero-width) after ~3s idle so the bar stays clean; ALSO fold in real download throughput when DownloadManager has active items (their receivedBytes deltas are exact — prefer real numbers over the io approximation while a file downloads). (c) both widgets: palette-only colors, accessibleName, tooltip explaining what they show + the approximation caveat; Linux-only /proc reads behind Q_OS_LINUX — hide the widgets on other platforms (no needs: tag — code-gated). (d) do NOT disturb the status-bar message area or the existing permanent widgets' order contract (new widgets go right of zoom — or left of loading; pick consistent with Vivaldi's left-anchored stats). Verify: a large page load shows nonzero down-rate then auto-hides; a heavy tab reads plausible MB that changes on allocation churn (e.g. opening a big site moves it); tab switch updates the number; renderer kill -> '—'; make check PASS.

### CONT06

Two-level container tab strip — user request (Vivaldi screenshot): when a window has container-bound tabs, don't show them inline with chips — show a SECOND tab row. Level 1 = one header entry per container present in the window (icon + name + tab-count badge like the screenshot's 'Example Domain [2]', plus a default entry for unbound tabs — 'Tabs' or window glyph); level 2 = the tabs of the currently-selected container only — all tab ops (reorder, close, context menu, middle-click) work on the filtered row. Clicking a level-1 header swaps level 2's contents; the active header gets selected styling; count badges update live on open/close/move. Drag a level-2 tab onto a different level-1 header -> rebind (reopenInContainer semantics — new tab in target container, old closes); drag between level-1 headers reorders them. Unbound tabs live under the default header — NO tab may be unreachable in either direction (a container with 0 tabs drops its header unless pinned-by-setting; the default header always exists). Interactions to preserve: TABGRP01 groups nest INSIDE a container level (container > group > tab hierarchy — a group's pill/chip renders within level 2 of its container), TABS01 vertical positions keep working (two-level renders stacked — level-1 row above level-2 row when the bar is vertical), the CONT02 per-tab chip may be dropped inside level-2 rows (redundant there) but kept in single-row mode. Mode choice: 'Container display: Inline chips / Two-level strip' setting on the Containers settings section (CONT03 if landed, else Tab Settings) — two-level can be the default when containers exist if it tests well; single-row stays available. Persist the active level-1 selection per window; new-window restore sane (last-used container or default). Accessibility: headers have names + counts in accessibleName, keyboard-focusable, arrow-key nav across levels. Verify: two containers' tabs never mix in one row, count badges exact, drag-across-containers rebinds, empty container header disappears, groups render inside their container's row, vertical + horizontal positions both sane, make check PASS.

### TABS04

(c) VERTICAL BAR — TABS02's vertical rows render pinned tabs identically at top, still icon-only or short-title (pick readable). (d) PERSIST — pin state rides the session record (RCORE03 format carries a per-tab flag — coordinate: if the session schema lacks a pin field, add it versioned so old blobs still decode; the engine-opaque blob is NOT the place — pin is shell state, goes in the structured record). (e) groups/containers coexist: pinned + group + container all defined together (pinned sorts before its group section). Verify: pin/unpin via menu, icon-only rendering, close-others skips pinned, middle-click ignores pinned, order persists after restart via session restore, works in vertical mode, make check PASS.

### TABS05

Undo closed tab (Ctrl+Shift+T) — user request: reopen recently closed tabs. Spec: (a) per-window closed-tab STACK (cap ~25): on tab close push a record {url, title, icon, index, engine-state blob (the same serialized per-tab state RCORE03 stores — restore scroll+history not just url), group id, container binding, pinned flag}; close-window-with-all-tabs pushes ONE record carrying the window's whole tab set so 'reopen closed window' works too if cheap (else single-tab records only — document). (b) UX — Ctrl+Shift+T reopens the most recent record AT ITS ORIGINAL INDEX (clamped), restores engine state + group/container/pin; the Tab menu (or tab context menu) gets 'Reopen Closed Tab' + a small submenu listing recent closed tabs with title+icon (pick N latest). (c) privacy: private windows never push to the stack (off-the-record means off-the-stack — and the stack is MEMORY-ONLY, never serialized to disk — a persisted undo stack is a history leak); clearing private data (Ctrl+Shift+Del paths) clears the stack. (d) edge cases: restored tab lands in the same container/group if those still exist (container deleted meanwhile -> open as default), undo of a pinned tab restores pinned state, repeated undo walks the stack in LIFO order. Verify: close 3 tabs -> 3x Ctrl+Shift+T restores all in order with history+scroll; index/group/container/pin round-trip; private tabs never appear; stack clears on private-data wipe; make check PASS.

### CONT07

Link context menu: private + tor open options — user request: right-click on a link should offer 'Open in New Tab', 'Open in New Window', 'Open in New PRIVATE Tab', 'Open in New PRIVATE Window', 'Open in New TOR Window'. Today webview.cpp:236-243 has only New Window + New Tab. USER DIRECTIVE: keep it as secure as possible — every new entry is a cross-profile hand-off of attacker-influenced linkUrl(), so treat each path as an untrusted-input boundary. Spec: (a) add the three entries under the existing two — order: New Tab, New Window, New Private Tab, New Private Window, New Tor Window (submenu 'Open In >' if the list feels long — implementer's call, default flat is fine); (b) wiring: private tab -> the same private-profile path as TabWidget::newPrivateTab/makeNewPrivateTab (~444-490) but loading the link URL; private window -> a new BrowserMainWindow on the privateWebEngineProfile then navigate; tor window -> reuse m_fileNewTorWindowAction's creation path (browsermainwindow ~1262, BrowserApplication::openTorWindow browserapplication.cpp:1089) so the new window is a REAL tor window (separate --tor process, tor profile + tor status + circuit label), not a themed normal one — EXTEND openTorWindow to carry the URL: pass it as a SEPARATE argv element in the QProcess::startDetached arg list (NEVER a shell string), and the receiving --tor process must route the URL through the same untrusted-input gate as any other external arg before loading — a javascript:/data: URL arriving over argv must be refused, not executed in a fresh chrome context; (c) UNTRUSTED-URL GATE — all five paths funnel through the same scheme discipline openUrlInTarget already uses (webview.cpp:524-539): every non-http(s) scheme must pass isUrlAllowedOnUntrustedInput (SEC02) — a javascript:/data:/blob: link right-clicked into any of the five targets opens nothing; (d) REFERER DISCIPLINE — openUrlInTarget attaches Referer=<current page> for http(s) loads (webview.cpp:533-535): only attach it when source profile == target profile (same-context opens keep hotlink-compat, matching a plain click); private-tab/private-window/tor opens from a different profile send NO Referer — it would leak the source page URL across a privacy boundary; (e) CONTEXT SENSITIVITY — inside a private window the two private entries collapse to 'New Tab/Window' semantics honestly (already private); inside a TOR window: 'New Private Tab'/'New Private Window' must NEVER land on the shared clearnet OTR profile — hide them or map them to tor-tab/tor-window semantics (a tor-window link opens in tor context or another tor window — never leak to clearnet profile), 'New Tor Window' works, plain New Tab/Window stay tor-bound (verify, don't break); no scriptable window.opener bridge may survive across profiles — the opened page must not get a handle back to the source page across contexts; (f) CONTAINERS — if the tab is container-bound, New Tab/Window follow the same container semantics existing open paths use (don't silently strip container binding); private/tor targets never inherit a container binding (OTR profiles have none); (g) also honor the same entries on IMAGE links (mediaUrl) if it falls out naturally — links are the requirement, images nice-to-have. Verify: all five entries present and land the link in the right context (private tab is OTR — nothing in history/icons/cookies; tor window actually a --tor process and tor-routed); tor window's private options can't escape to clearnet; javascript:/data: links refused on all five paths; Referer absent on cross-profile opens; make check PASS.

### CONT08

Link-open security audit + regression tests — user directive 'keep it as secure as possible': adversarial verification pass over CONT07's cross-context open paths. ### TPREV02

Hover preview shows wrong content for non-active tabs — user report: 'the preview is not correct when I move the mouse over a non active tab.' Root-cause direction (verify first): TabWidget::tabThumbnail (tabwidget.cpp:1983) grabs the hidden view's compositor frame — the code comments admit 'a hidden WebEngine view usually still paints its last compositor frame on grab()' — for a long-hidden background tab that frame is STALE (shows old scroll/content, or worse a leftover frame from a different page); blankThumbnailFrame() only catches fully-blank grabs so a stale-but-nonblank frame passes and renders as the wrong preview. Suspects to check: stale frame vs m_tabThumbnails cache ordering (a fresh grab that's stale replaces a good cached thumb at :1970), sleeping/discarded tabs showing the pre-discard snapshot vs current state (tabpreview.cpp:114 note), thumbnails keyed by WebView* not tab (a recycled view could show another tab's image). Task: make the preview show the tab's actual current page — prefer the cached per-tab thumbnail (updated on real load/paint events, e.g. the refresh at :2329) over request-time grabs for hidden views; only live-grab for the ACTIVE tab; discard frames that can't be proven current (track frame/page identity — url+load-generation on the stored thumb); on miss show title+url text card, never another tab's pixels. Verify: open A, switch to B, hover A -> shows A's real content not B's and not stale; suspended tab shows its snapshot; recycled webview doesn't leak a previous tab's image; tst legs + negative control on the stale-frame path; make check PASS. needs:task:TPREV01

### PREFUI09

Privacy page group alignment — user screenshot: inside Preferences > Privacy, the Cookies group shows its label+combo+button rows pushed to the RIGHT side of the group while the 'Filter Tracking Cookies' and 'Block third-party cookies' checkboxes sit flush LEFT — the group's controls are not aligned. Diagnosis hint: same class of bug as the Performance Startup spacer fix — check settings.ui for these groups' layouts (cookiejar settings block, site permissions block, saved-passwords block): likely an expanding horizontal spacer or a grid form whose label column absorbs all width pushing field cells right, while checkbox rows were added outside that form alignment.

Spec: (a) Cookies group — Accept Cookies / Keep Cookies Until / 'A cookie session ends' label+combo+button rows aligned consistently with the group (left-anchored form like other Preferences pages — match the AutoFill or General page field alignment); (b) the two checkboxes align with the same left edge; (c) Site Permissions group — same alignment pass; (d) Saved Passwords group — same alignment pass; (e) sweep the whole Privacy page for any other group with the same leading-spacer/right-push defect.

Verify: all three groups' controls share the group's left margin like other pages; combos do not stretch the full page width beyond their siblings; make check PASS (tst_settingsdialog if it covers these pages).

DONE (2026-10-12): root cause — a QGridLayout item that spans the column holding the stretch factor breaks the stretch: surplus went into column 0 (cookies: the two checkbox rows spanned all 3 cols -> label column ballooned to 375px, right-aligned labels + combos + buttons all floated right) or rendered the whole content centered in a narrow band (permissions: hint+tree spanned the stretch col; passwords: status label spanned it). Fix: cookies checkbox rows now span cols 0-1 only and every combo/button cell carries alignment=Qt::AlignLeft; permissionsGroupBox and credentialGroupBox were re-based to QVBoxLayout (hint label + tree full width; button rows are hbox + trailing expanding spacer), matching the Connections & Storage / Fingerprint Resistance / Site Trust convention. Sweep of the rest of the page found no other group with the defect (Content Blocking's spanning rows avoid its stretch column; all others are vbox/hbox). Verified: tst_SettingsDialog::privacyGroupAlignment locks label-column width, shared left edges, packed buttons and resize stability; offscreen page grab confirmed; tst_settingsdialog 41/41; --settings-smoke PASS; make check rc=0 (check-warnings PASS, 65 programs 0 failed) offscreen --disable-gpu.

### RSZ02

Sidebar and tab strip are not mouse-resizable — user report: 'the Side bar should be resizeable by me with mouse. Same for the TABs.' Same for the tab strip's edge; (b) find why the grip is inert — suspects: grip widget zero-width/invisible (RSZ01 made it 'slim' — maybe 0px), grip behind the panel (z-order), mouse events swallowed by the tab/panel children, resizeFinished connected but the width setter clamps back, or the dock minimum/maximum widths pin the range; (c) make the resize REAL: a visible hover affordance (cursor change + a few-px hot zone), live drag resizing persisted via persistSidebarWidth; tab strip same contract if it is meant to be height-resizable (if the design only allows sidebar resize, make the sidebar work first and document the tab choice); (d) keep RSZ01's persistence + dock-area mirroring.

TabBar generalized to all four shapes: `inResizeGrip` = ~8px content-edge band (West right, East left, North bottom, South top; header-band y<0 excluded), hover tracking + `paintResizeGrip` divider/handle, SizeHor/SizeVer cursor, grip press returns before tab selection/move-drag arming, release persists + live-fans-out to other TabBars, double-click resets (vertical 180, horizontal 0 = Automatic). ### TABS08

Tab strip still too tall — user report after TABS07 (strip 30->27px, close button qBound(12,fm,16), tabSizeHint clamp): 'the Tab size is still the same.' TABS07's fix landed but the visible tab height did not change for the user. Measure the ACTUAL rendered tab-button height (widget grab / rect dump), not the strip container — suspects the clamp landed on a path the tab bar does not use (custom TabBar class vs QTabBar::tabSizeHint, a different height source: font metrics + style padding, PM_TabBarTabVSpace, minimumSectionSize, the vertical-tab layout, or a stylesheet min-height). Done (2026-10-12): geometry probes on the real BrowserMainWindow showed the TABS07 clamp does drive tabRect height (fm=17 @11pt -> 29px, inside its cap — TABS07 only shaved ~4px there). The real inflation was CONT02's container name chip: containerChipSize() added chipHeight+3 (~18px) to a container tab's hint and QTabBar lays out every tab at the tallest hint, so a single container tab under the reporter's containerDisplay=0 grew the whole strip 29->47px. Fix in src/tabbar.cpp: chip row no longer reserved (containerChipSize returns 0 height on horizontal bars, 3px width stripe only on vertical) — container tabs render at the shared thickness with the accent edge stripe painted inside the tab rect and the container name on the tooltip; the group pill re-anchored to the row's own reserved space; auto thickness tightened to fm+8 floor / max(fm,iconSize)+9 cap -> measured 26px bar at 11pt, uniform tabRects, container tab adds no height; user-set tabs/horizontalTabHeight override untouched; tor window strip is the same TabBar + a stylesheet with no min-height, so it clamps to the identical bound. Regression: new tst_BrowserMainWindow::tabStripCompactHeight (desktop font, uniform-height assert, container-tab no-growth, close-button centering, optional ARORA_STRIP_GRAB_DIR grab); containermanager containerChipIndicator now asserts equal strip height + painted stripe; tst_tabbar tabStripHeight bounds updated. make check rc=0 — check-warnings PASS, 67 programs 0 failed, offscreen --disable-gpu. (Pre-existing flake noted: --tabstrip-smoke's live-thumbnail/pre-sleep-snapshot capture checks fail on unmodified HEAD too.)

Hardening: new `TorManager::controlConnected()` (socket liveness, not manager state); `requestCircuitInfo` re-checks the link inside the circuit-status callback before queueing stream-status and resets `m_circuitQueryInFlight`/`m_streamCircuitId` on loss; the control `disconnected` handler clears both in-flight flags; `resolveHopCountries` re-checks before ip-to-country; `TorControl` dtor disconnects the socket and drains pending callbacks; `populateMenu` shows an honest "Tor circuit unavailable — control connection lost" when Ready-but-disconnected (no stale hop rows); flagIcon now requires exactly two ASCII A-Z before building regional indicators. ### TOR11

Tor window TITLE BAR not colored — user report: 'the title line of the Tor window is not colored like the menu, url.' TOR07 unified menu/header/tab-strip coloring with the URL-bar treatment, but the OS window title bar itself stays default. Native title bars are WM-drawn — the app cannot tint them directly. Investigate the honest options in order: (a) does the platform honor a dark/colored title variant (GTK _GTK_THEME_VARIANT=dark, xdg decoration hints, KDE color-scheme dbus) — if a cheap hint exists that makes the WM draw a matching dark/tor-toned title bar, use it for tor windows only; (b) if no native path works on this host, weigh a client-side custom title (Qt::FramelessWindowHint + drawn title bar with min/max/close + window drag) — heavier, only if (a) fails; document which was chosen and why. Verify: tor window title area visually matches the tor chrome (menu/url coloring) on this host's WM; non-tor windows unchanged; window still movable/resizable/closable; make check PASS.

Done (2026-10-12): option (a) is not available on this host — GNOME Wayland: Qt 6.12's own Adwaita client-side decoration only follows the session-wide `org.freedesktop.appearance color-scheme` portal key (no per-window tint API), and `_GTK_THEME_VARIANT` is X11-only — so (b) was built. Tor windows only (non-macOS) go `Qt::FramelessWindowHint` and the menubar row doubles as the title bar, headerbar-style: `QMenuBar::setCornerWidget` puts a `TorTitleBar` (Tor badge + live window title, drag surface) in the left corner and min/max/close painted glyphs in the right; empty menubar space drags via `QWindow::startSystemMove` (manual-move fallback where the platform lacks it, e.g. offscreen — same trick as PipWindow) and double-click toggles maximize; the window's violet frame ring (contentsMargins) doubles as the edge-resize grab zone via `startSystemResize`. The real QMenuBar stays in the QMainWindow menu slot — a wrapper in that slot is evicted by the next `menuBar()` call, which lazily creates a fresh empty QMenuBar and loses the populated menus (found the hard way: first iteration used `setMenuWidget` and the bar vanished at show()). Menus keep working, full-screen/menubar-hide carry the controls away with the row, normal windows keep native decoration. Verified: grab shows violet menu-row-as-titlebar + frame ring; tst_BrowserMainWindow::torTitleBar (frameless flag, corner hosting, live title sync, violet pixels, max/restore ring collapse, manual drag moves window, minimize) 36/36 offscreen; --tor-window-smoke PASS on real tor (IsTor:true NAM+WebEngine); make check offscreen.

Vertical tab bar tabs are too big — user report with TWO screenshots: with Tab Bar Position Left (or Right), the strip eats a big chunk of window width AND each tab row is far too TALL (user confirms: 'not just too wide but too height' — rows render ~2x a single text line) — the user wants a compact Vivaldi-style rail by default. TABS02 shipped horizontal-label rows + a drag grip (tabs/verticalTabWidth, clamp 120-400, default 180) but the DEFAULT is still too wide and the rows too tall. Spec: (a) DEFAULT GEOMETRY — shrink the default verticalTabWidth to something favicon+short-title sized (aim ~120-140px — measure: favicon 16 + ~8px margins + room for a ~10-12 char elided title + close button; do NOT go below a readable row, and keep the 120px drag clamp floor). Row height should come off font metrics + padding like the horizontal strip (TABS07/08 bound ~fm+8..fm+12, ~26-30px — audit the vertical row height path in TabBar::tabSizeHint and shave any leftover padding/chip slack so a plain tab row is a single text line high). (b) FIRST-RUN/EXISTING USERS — a saved tabs/verticalTabWidth persists and must NOT be silently stomped, but users who never dragged the grip (still on the old default 180) should get the new compact default — detect an untouched value (key absent OR == 180) and migrate it; a user-set width stays. (c) TABS09's icon-only rail and group-chip label path must still size correctly at the smaller default; TABS04 pinned rows unaffected; container edge stripe (TABS08) still visible. (d) the drag grip + View/menu affordances unchanged; Top/Bottom positions unchanged. Verify: fresh profile with Left/Right position shows a compact readable rail (grab it and review), row height is one text line, icon-only + group chip + container stripe all still render right, drag resize still works and a user-set width persists across restart, make check PASS.

Done (2026-10-12): default tabs/verticalTabWidth 180 -> 140 — file-scope defaultVerticalTabWidth/legacyVerticalTabWidth constants now back the ctor seed, the grip double-click reset and reloadVerticalTabWidth, which treats an absent key or a stored 180 as untouched and migrates it to 140 while any other stored value (a real drag pick) stays; the 120-400 clamp is unchanged. Row-height audit found no slack: vertical rows already hint at fm.height()+8 (a single text line) and documentMode makes the production bar non-expanding, so nothing else pads them — plain rows stay single-line, named group-run first members keep their label band, container tabs keep the width-only 3px edge stripe, and the icon-only rail (iconSize+20 tile; group chip capped at the strip width) sizes correctly at the new default. Verified: new tst_TabBar::verticalTabWidthMigration (absent->140, stored 180->140, stored 260 kept, stored 140 round-trips through reload), new tst_TabWidget::verticalTabCompactDefault (fresh-profile 140 rail + real bar width/row height, 180 migration, 220 kept via loadSettings), new real-window leg in tst_BrowserMainWindow::tabStripCompactHeight (West position, 11pt font: row <= fm+12, bar ~140px, container stripe + close button render); verticalTabRows tightened to a single-line fm bound + env-gated ARORA_TABBAR_GRAB_DIR grab; verticalTabResize expectations re-anchored to the 140 start width; tst_tabbar 14/14, tst_tabwidget 20/20, tst_browsermainwindow 36/36, ARORA_TABBAR_GRAB_DIR + ARORA_STRIP_GRAB_DIR offscreen grabs reviewed (compact readable rail, single-line rows), make check: check-warnings PASS + all programs green except known unrelated flake adblockAppliesOnContainerProfile (PASS standalone) offscreen --disable-gpu.

### TOR12

Tor window title bar: the window/page title text is barely readable — user report with screenshot: next to the 'Tor' badge the title ('Welcome to Arora!') renders as a dim squeezed sliver against the violet chrome. TorTitleBar (src/tor/tortitlebar.cpp) puts m_titleLabel (SqueezeLabel, maxWidth 360) on the menubar corner; stylesheet only sets QLabel{color:#eee} — likely dimmed further by the menubar styling / palette and the squeeze. Make the title actually readable: proper contrasting color on the violet chrome (white or near-white — compute against the tor chrome background, not the menu item bg), don't let it get squeezed when there's room (it's a title — the menubar items should move first; check the corner-widget width negotiation: maxWidth 360 + squeeze elide may starve it when the menubar takes the space), keep the drag surface + badge + controls working, keep 'Arora — ' style titles updating on currentChanged (the setText hook at windowTitleChanged already exists). Watch: arora-dark theme — the violet chrome stays violet so title stays light either way, but verify. Verify: title legible at a glance in light + dark themes, long titles elide rather than clip mid-glyph, grab reviewed, make check PASS.

Done (2026-10-12): two real defects behind the "dim squeezed sliver". (1) SqueezeLabel elides by rewriting its own text(), which shrinks the sizeHint the menubar's corner-widget negotiation reads (QMenuBarPrivate::updateGeometries assigns the corner exactly sizeHint()) — one squeezed pass collapsed the corner permanently and destroyed the original title. Replaced with TorTitleLabel (src/tor/tortitlebar.cpp): keeps the real title in m_fullTitle, reports the FULL title width as sizeHint (menus move right, capped at 420 so a huge title can't push menus into the overflow extension), swaps the middle-elided form into text() only for display, and carries the full title on the tooltip — shrinking regrows, nothing is destroyed. (2) The menubar only renegotiates the corner width when its items are dirty — a title change doesn't reach that pass, so the corner kept the previous title's width; new syncCornerWidth() feeds the menubar a synthetic resize on WindowTitleChange so the new sizeHint claims its room at once. Readability: #torTitleText goes pure white (~12:1 vs #4a3372 — the violet chrome stays violet under arora-dark so light ink is correct in both schemes). Verified: new tst_BrowserMainWindow::torTitleBarTitle (short title full+unclipped, long title elides at cap with tooltip intact + File menu renegotiated after the corner, shrink-back regrowth — the destructive-squeeze regression, near-white ink + zero dark pixels in label grabs under light AND dark app palettes), tst_browsermainwindow 37/37, ARORA_TOR_GRAB_DIR grab reviewed (bright white title), make check: check-warnings PASS + all programs green except known unrelated flake fileSchemeErrors (PASS standalone, 159ms) offscreen --disable-gpu.

### TOR13

Tor circuit popup drifts right continuously — user report: clicking the tor circuit button shows the circuit fine, but the popover keeps sliding to the right. Root cause is almost certainly the live-refresh loop in TorCircuitButton (src/tor/torcircuitbutton.cpp): every TorManager::circuitsChanged while the menu is visible calls populateMenu() -> m_menu->clear() + rebuild + requestCircuitInfo() — and populateMenu() itself calls requestCircuitInfo() which triggers ANOTHER circuitsChanged on reply, so the menu repopulates in a loop; each clear()+re-add on a VISIBLE QMenu makes Qt re-layout/reposition the popup and on this DE it creeps right each cycle. Fix: break the loop AND stop re-laying-out a visible menu — (a) do NOT call requestCircuitInfo() from inside a circuitsChanged-driven repopulate (only kick the re-query on aboutToShow; the signal path must be refresh-only, not re-request), and (b) if the visible-menu repopulate still repositions after the loop is gone, rebuild into a fresh action list and only swap contents if they actually changed (compare hop rows before clearing — flag icons/names/fps), or switch to a QTimer-deferred repopulate; last resort keep the menu static while open and refresh on next open (user still sees a live circuit — opened data stays correct because aboutToShow re-queries). Watch TOR08's instant-popup contract + the 'circuit unavailable' states; TOR10's crash-hardening gates must stay. Verify: open the dropdown on a live tor window, menu stays pinned to the button and does NOT creep even while circuit updates arrive (leave it open 10s+ through at least one circuitsChanged); circuit rows still reflect a fresh snapshot; --tor-circuit-click-smoke cycles still 0 crashes; make check PASS.

Done (2026-10-12): refactored TorCircuitButton onto a row-descriptor model (CircuitMenuRow + torCircuitMenuRows/applyCircuitRows — addCircuitRows keeps its public contract for tests). populateMenu (aboutToShow) still kicks requestCircuitInfo + rebuilds; the circuitsChanged/stateChanged path is the new syncOpenMenu — refresh-only, never queries, and skips the rebuild entirely when circuitRowSignature matches what the menu already shows, so a visible QMenu is never re-laid out on no-op updates (the creep mechanism). TOR10's controlConnected gates and all honest state strings preserved. faketor.py now logs every GETINFO circuit-status to <datadir>/circuit-status-log. Verified: new tst_TorManager::circuitMenuNoRequeryLoop (menu shows fresh Guard row, manual circuitsChanged/stateChanged emit leaves action pointers + menu->pos() identical, 1.5s dwell adds zero circuit-status queries — the pre-fix loop would have logged dozens); --tor-circuit-click-smoke PASS 74 cycles faketor / 315 cycles real tor 0 crashes honest dead-control state; tst_tormanager 14/14+1skip, tst_browsermainwindow 39/39, make check rc=0 (check-warnings PASS, 65 suites 0 failed) offscreen --disable-gpu.

### DOWN04

Downloads sidebar: finished download vanishes + header buttons clipped — user report with screenshot: (a) 'The last downloaded file disappears from the download list when finished downloading. It should be there until I clear out the list.' (b) 'I can not see the main action button' — the header row shows a search field + a button cluster that is clipped/squashed (pause/globe/dash/broom glyphs partially visible at the row's right edge). For (a): the user's removeDownloadsPolicy is already Never, so the disappearance is NOT the policy — check the OTHER removal paths: DownloadManager::updateRow removes !downloading && m_offTheRecord rows unconditionally (OTR downloads auto-vanish on finish — if the user downloaded in a private tab this fires by design; decide whether the sidebar should still show finished private rows until cleared — the privacy-safe read is they SHOULD vanish, so first REPRODUCE on a normal download to confirm which path actually fires — could also be a proxy/sort issue where the row is still in the model but filtered/blank in the sidebar view: SidebarDownloadsModel is a QSortFilterProxyModel with search text + sort; a stale search filter or a sort that pushes the finished row out of view looks identical to 'disappeared'). Reproduce with a real download on a non-private tab; check m_downloadsModel row count before/after finish vs what the proxy shows. For (b): the downloads sidebar header row's button cluster is clipped — measure the row's available width vs the cluster's sizeHint (sidebar is resizable — RSZ02 — and the cluster must shrink/reflow instead of clipping; if these are new DLUX buttons, check minimum width + sizePolicy + whether the dock's minimum is narrower than the cluster). Verify: finish a download on a normal tab — the row stays visible in the list until Clear is used; private-tab downloads follow the documented OTR rule (auto-vanish) and that rule is re-confirmed intentional + noted in the task notes; header buttons fully visible at the sidebar's minimum width and on resize; make check PASS.

Done (2026-10-12): (a) REPRODUCED via new tst_BrowserMainWindow::downloadsSidebarFinishedRows — a normal-profile data: download finishes and STAYS in both DownloadManager::model() and the sidebar proxy (rowCount 1, CompletedRole true, selection maps back to the live DownloadItem, explicit removeItem clears it). The 'disappearing finished download' the user saw was the OTR leg — reproduced with a trickle-fed QTcpServer download on an off-the-record page: the row appears while downloading and auto-vanishes on stop() through the `!downloading && m_offTheRecord` branch in updateRow(). Re-confirmed as the documented SEC07 privacy rule (OTR downloads are also not persisted to QSettings) — no normal-row removal bug exists in the source model or the proxy. (b) Root cause measured: the expanded DownloadItem card demanded ~442px minimum (fixed horizontal button rows + full-width SqueezeLabel sizeHints) vs the dock's 160px clampedWidth floor — the detail card crushed buttons to slivers and the header cluster clipped. Restructured src/downloaditem.ui to a vertical card (icon+filename+expand top row; progress/info middle; details block; single cardButtonsLayout of all five actions), set every text label to QSizePolicy::Ignored so SqueezeLabel elides instead of demanding width, and DownloadItem swaps cardButtonsLayout for a new wrapping FlowLayout (src/utils/flowlayout.{h,cpp}, added to src.pri) so buttons reflow onto extra rows; header widgets got Ignored/Minimum policies so the sort combo and buttons shrink to fit instead of clipping. New tst downloadsSidebarMinWidth exercises the dock at/below its floor asserting header controls + card action buttons stay inside bounds. ### PREFUI12

Privacy page Cookies section alignment — user report with screenshot: 'cookies section is left alligned.' On Preferences -> Privacy, the Cookies block's controls sit on a different/ragged left edge than the rest of the page: the 'Accept Cookies' label+combo+Exceptions... row and the 'Keep Cookies Until' label+combo+Cookies... row are pushed right of the checkbox column (Block Popup Windows / Filter Tracking Cookies / Block third-party cookies), are not column-aligned with EACH OTHER (the two combos/buttons start at different x), and the 'A cookie session ends' row sits at yet another offset — the whole section reads visually broken next to the Security Level combo and Web Content checkboxes above it. Fix in settings.ui (+ settings.cpp only if behavior needs it): put the Cookies rows on the page's normal control geometry — same left edge as sibling sections, the two combo rows sharing one form-column alignment (labels left-aligned as a column, combos aligned, trailing buttons adjacent per PREFUI10's field-bounds convention — capped widths, no page-edge stretch), 'cookie session ends' row aligned likewise. It's almost certainly a stray indent/nested hbox in the .ui — prefer removing the extra nesting over adding spacers. Verify: offscreen grab of the Privacy page — Cookies rows share the left edge with Security Level/Web Content and their combos/buttons column-align; resize to min width — no clipping; tst_settingsdialog PASS; make check PASS.

Done (2026-10-12): root cause — the three cookie form labels (Accept Cookies: / Keep Cookies Until: / A cookie session ends:) were the only labels on the Privacy page carrying Qt::AlignRight|AlignTrailing, so inside the ~130px form column each label's text started at a different x and floated the whole row right of the checkbox/sibling-section left edge (Search/Tab-Settings/Proxy pages also use right-aligned labels, but there every label in the group shares the style — internally coherent). Fix: all three flipped to Qt::AlignLeft|Qt::AlignVCenter matching the page's other labels (adblockEngineLabel, referer/DoH labels) — label text, checkbox rows and sibling groups now share one left edge while combos keep their form column and buttons their action column; no layout reshaping needed (PREFUI09 had already fixed the stretch-column leak). privacyGroupAlignment extended to lock the left-align flag on all three labels. Verified: offscreen grabs at 900x1600 and 640x1200 — cookies rows share the left edge, combos/buttons column-aligned, no clipping at min width; tst_settingsdialog 42/42; make check: check-warnings PASS + all suites green except the 2 documented pre-existing clean-HEAD flakes torChromeTheme + colorSchemeApply (unrelated theme-palette asserts), offscreen --disable-gpu.

### TABS11

Vertical tab rows still too tall — user re-report with screenshot ('The tab size still not good. the height is too big'): TABS07 cut the horizontal strip 30->27px, TABS08 killed the container-chip second row (->~26px), TABS10 shrank the vertical width 180->140 and AUDITED row height claiming fm+8 — yet on the user's real desktop the vertical rail rows still render visibly taller than the horizontal strip's ~26px (screenshot shows 'Untitled'/'Welcome...' rows with roomy padding). Do NOT trust the audit — MEASURE on a real window (grab + tabRect dump): actual painted row height vs horizontal strip vs font metrics; find what feeds the extra pixels (forced-North style option padding in TABS02's vertical paint path? documentMode margins? icon size + spacing constants? minimumSizeHint on the rail host?). Target: vertical row height == horizontal strip height (~26px, fm+~10) — visually tight like Vivaldi. Verify: real-window grab showing vertical rows matching the horizontal strip's compact height, tst_tabbar/tst_tabwidget updated if they pin the old geometry, make check PASS.

Done (2026-10-12): MEASURED on a real window, not audited — rows were already fm+8 (tabRect (0,0,143x25)/(0,25,143x25) at fm=17, matching the 26px horizontal strip) and expanding=false via documentMode, so the geometry was never the problem. The real defect: a ~46px ghost block painted below the SELECTED row (grab bisection: vanished with tab draws skipped, persisted over a full-widget magenta prefill, no child widget covers it). Root cause is a Qt 6.12 Fusion-style quirk in CE_TabBarTabShape — for a selected tab it runs fillRect(x+1, rect.bottom()-1, w-2, rect.bottom()-1, tabFrameColor): the 4th arg is HEIGHT but receives the rect's absolute bottom coordinate. On a ~26px horizontal strip the overdraw lands outside the widget (harmless); on a 600px-tall West/East rail the selected row at depth y grows a (y+height-1)px tab-colored block — the "roomy padding"/"rows too tall" the user saw, worst for the last tab. Requires the QTabWidget parent (HasFrame -> tabFrameColor != window), which is why the standalone-bar unit tests could never see it. Fix: painter.setClipRect(option.rect) around the two hand-painted CE_TabBarTab draws in the vertical branch (rows + floating move-drag row) — the CONT06 horizontal banded path is left unclipped (same convention as QTabBar::paintEvent, and the clip would eat Fusion's intentional tabOverlap wing). Regression leg added to tst_BrowserMainWindow::tabStripCompactHeight: selects the last tab, grabs the rail, asserts the pixel below the last row equals the strip tail — verified it FAILS with the clip disabled (ff434343 ghost vs ff353535 tail). Verified: fresh real-window grab shows two clean ~25px rows, no ghost; tst_tabbar 14/14 (+1 skip), tst_tabwidget 20/20 (+9 skips), tst_browsermainwindow 42+1 (torChromeTheme, documented clean-HEAD flake), check-warnings PASS, make check rc=1 only from the documented pre-existing flakes torChromeTheme + colorSchemeApply + clearSiteData offscreen --disable-gpu.

### SPAGE01

Start page renders as horizontal bands — user report with screenshot ('the start page have lines and it not looks like one page with colors'): qrc:/startpage.html shows three visibly distinct horizontal bands behind the header/search/footer column instead of one coherent page background. src/htmls/startpage.css body uses `linear-gradient(to bottom, #ccc 0%, #fff 50%, #fff 100%) repeat-x` — a top->middle gradient that under dark-scheme tagging (THEME01's arora-dark handling) reads as banded stripes; the bands may also be the gradient's hard stops rendering unblended at this viewport height, or the -tor/-private/-servo variants inheriting the same structure. Diagnose which layer produces each visible band (body gradient vs a band element vs dark-scheme recolor), then restyle so the page reads as ONE continuous surface — either a single-page gradient that covers the full document height (html+body 100% height, no repeat seam) or a flat scheme-aware background; keep the branded header/search/footer composition intact and mirror the fix across startpage{,-private,-tor,-servo}.css so all variants share the treatment. Verify: offscreen/real-window grabs of all four variants — no horizontal banding, coherent page color in both light and dark schemes, make check PASS.

Done (2026-10-12): DIAGNOSED by rendering each variant offscreen through a stub-arora-channel WebEngine tool (printToPdf + pdftoppm + pixel seam scan). The band layer is the BODY-propagated canvas background: WebEngine sizes the background image tile to the body's box (~250px) and tiles it down the canvas — every ~250px the gradient restarts, giving the "lines". Light scheme hides it because the gradient tail is canvas-white; the arora-dark override (#2a2a30->#1e1e23) makes each restart a visible seam (measured at y=250/501/751 on the 841pt render). The -tor/-private variants were already clean (TOR09/PTAB02 put the gradient on html{min-height:100%}); -servo was clean but carried a dead repeat-x. Fix: startpage.css now puts the page gradient on html{min-height:100%} for both schemes (html.arora-dark keeps the dark gradient on <html>, body keeps only the ink colors), and servo's dead repeat-x is gone — all four variants share the treatment. Regression leg: tst_BrowserMainWindow::startPageSingleSurface loads all four qrc pages and asserts computed style — documentElement carries the gradient at full-viewport height while body has background-image:none. Verified: pdfgrab pixel scan of all 8 renders (4 variants x light/dark) shows zero seams and one coherent surface; new leg + torStartPage/servoStartPage/privateStartPage PASS; check-warnings PASS; make check rc=1 only from the two documented clean-HEAD flakes (torChromeTheme, colorSchemeApply).

### NAVB01

Stop/Reload state never propagates to chrome — user reports TWO symptoms, likely one broken signal path: (a) 'the reload button should have a stop function as well. When it is loading it should change to stop button, when the load failed or done it should work as a reload' — the toolbar's m_stopReloadAction stays reload during loads; (b) 'the icons are not changing on the url bar (stop or reload)' — the location-bar reload/stop affordance doesn't flip either. The machinery EXISTS: m_stopReloadAction swaps icon + triggered-target on BrowserMainWindow::loadProgress (~2589 — wired to m_viewStopAction when 0<progress<100, back to m_viewReloadAction otherwise), fed by TabWidget::loadProgress <- Engine::Page::loadProgress (tabwidget.cpp ~337 re-wires on tab change). Since BOTH chrome surfaces fail, suspect upstream: (1) Engine::Page::loadProgress is never emitted in the Qt6 port (engine-adapter signal never wired — ENG04 refactor territory; check whether QWebEnginePage::loadProgress still reaches Engine::Page's emit on webengine backend, and what servo/fake backends do); (2) TabWidget's forwarding connect is established for the wrong tab/never (check the connect counterpart of the :337 disconnect — was it lost in a merge?); (3) progress arrives but the icon flip is fine while the STOP path is dead (m_viewStopAction -> Engine::StandardAction::Stop binding). Diagnose first — instrument where the chain dies (log/assert each hop) then fix: load -> icon becomes stop glyph + click stops; finish OR fail -> back to reload + click reloads. Also cover the URL-bar icon surface (locationbar's own reload/stop widget — find where it's meant to update; probably the same enginePage loadProgress/loadFinished signals). Also a THIRD reported symptom in the same family — 'the backward button, sometimes not doing anything': m_historyBackAction (addWebAction -> Engine::StandardAction::Back) intermittently dead, most plausibly the same page-action/state binding decay (action never re-enabled when history state changes, or triggered() reaches a stale/foreign page after tab swaps — check how Back binds per-tab in the currentChanged path and whether engine tabs steal the binding). Diagnose all three against the shared binding/signal path rather than patching each symptom. Engine tabs too if the affordance shows there. Verify: real page load — toolbar button shows stop icon + stops the load mid-flight; done/error restores reload; url-bar icon flips in the same moments; private/tor windows same; tst leg pinning the flip; make check PASS.

Done (2026-10-13): DIAGNOSED end-to-end with a held-open QTcpServer fixture — the chain emits and delivers fine, but Qt 6.12's QWebEnginePage::loadProgress fires ONCE with 0 then once with 100; no mid-flight value ever arrives, so every surface keyed on 0<progress<100 (toolbar flip, url-bar gradient cue, LoadingIndicator mid-attach) was structurally dead. Fix mirrors the LoadingIndicator pattern (the one surface that worked, because it already used loadStarted/loadFinished): (a) new TabWidget::loadStarted/loadFinished(bool) forward the CURRENT page's Engine::Page lifecycle, re-wired in currentChanged alongside the existing loadProgress re-wire; (b) BrowserMainWindow::updateStopReloadState(bool) flips the combined action — Stop face + dispatch to m_viewStopAction while loading, Reload face + m_viewReloadAction otherwise (Qt::UniqueConnection) — loadProgress() keeps honoring real mid-flight values for backends that emit them, with enginePage()->isLoading() consulted at progress 0/100; (c) new src/locationbar/stopreloadbutton.{h,cpp} — QToolButton at the url bar's right end, same face rule, click dispatches enginePage()->stop()/reload(), mid-load attach reads WebView::isLoading() (new out-of-line accessor — WebPage incomplete in webview.h); LocationBar paints a thin loading strip on its bottom edge while isLoading() since the real-progress gradient no longer has values to draw; (d) LoadingIndicator::setWebView mid-attach check moved from 0<progress<100 to enginePage()->isLoading(). Back leg: WebActionMapper::rootTriggered dispatches once — verified enabled after two navigations and exactly one history step per trigger (earlier 'double-step' observation was session-history mangling from an error-page commit landing Back on a held URL, not the mapper). Verified: extended tst_BrowserMainWindow::stopReloadState exercises held-load Stop face on BOTH surfaces -> real stop -> finish/fail -> real reload (fresh server hit) -> two-clean-nav Back single step -> tab switch away/back onto a loading tab -> private-window leg -> tor-window leg (60s SOCKS-failure timeout, no daemon); 3/3 green x3 runs no flakes, 31s incl. tor leg; check-warnings PASS (zero diags, clean resumed tree); make check rc=1 only from the 2 documented clean-HEAD flakes torChromeTheme + colorSchemeApply (both FAIL standalone, same as prior tasks); also fixed a pre-existing -Wignored-qualifiers warning in utils/scopeshortcuts.cpp:28 (const on return type) that the zero-warning gate rightly rejected. Note: resumed ARORA_WARN_BUILD tree needed the prebuilt libarora_adblock_ffi.a copied in (adblock.pri links it with no cargo rule, unlike rustcore/rustdl).

### TABS13

Tab tear-off to new window + cross-window merge — user request: 'drag the tab and move it out of the tab list and when I release it will work as a new window. Also if I drag a separate arora window [tab] then I should be able to put it back into any windows tab list.' Today TabBar's drag is a URL-ONLY drag (tabbar.cpp ~1020 — mimeData = url + text, 'action: tab-reordering'; dropEvent ~1099 just loads the url onto a tab/new tab). Spec: (a) MIME — tag real tab drags with an arora-specific mime type carrying source window id + source tab identity (keep url payload too for drops onto foreign apps/desktop — don't break dropping a tab into another app as a link); (b) TEAR-OFF — drop released OUTSIDE any tab bar (over desktop/other window body) detaches the actual tab into a fresh BrowserMainWindow: the real WebView moves (preserve page+history+scroll, NOT a re-navigated url) — reuse the tab-removal/insertion machinery; carry pin/group/container binding + TABS12 title override + PTAB01 OTR flag; empty-source window closes itself (or follows the quit-on-last-tab-close setting); (c) MERGE — drop an arora-tab drag onto another window's tab bar inserts the real tab at the drop index (move, not copy): remove from source, insert into target honoring pin-zone/group/container rules (drop onto pinned zone pins? follow TABS04's drop-position semantics), source window cleans up; a drop mid-strip over a tab row should insert there not load-onto that tab — the arora mime path takes precedence over the legacy url-drop only when the source is a real arora tab (same process, same profile family); (d) CONSTRAINTS — private/OTR tabs only merge into private windows (never leak into a persistent window); container tabs carry their container binding (level rules apply — a container the target can't host: land as normal tab or refuse honestly, document); tor windows are a separate process-global mode — no cross-mode merges (the tor drag just can't drop on non-tor bars); engine tabs (servo EngineTab) can't move (no cross-window transfer path — drop is refused); (e) drop-on-own-bar stays today's reorder behavior. Verify: tear-off creates a new window with live page+history intact; merge moves the tab into the target at the drop index and it's removed from source; OTR boundary enforced; pin/group state survives; url-drag-to-foreign-app still works; tst legs where drivable + real drag smoke if a harness exists; make check PASS.

Done note (2026-10-13): implemented as specced. (a) application/x-arora-tab carries "<source TabBar>:<page widget>" and is only trusted when it matches the live in-process drag session (s_tabDragSource/s_tabDragPage) — stale or foreign payloads can never reach a tab; url/text stay set so drops into other apps still hand over the link. (b) An unhandled release (exec()==IgnoreAction, drop not consumed) runs TabWidget::tearTabToNewWindow — newMainWindow + moveTabTo at index 0, then the ctor's blank placeholder is closed (or discarded from the hidden container store via closeHiddenTab when the fresh two-level strip filed the moved tab under its own level; the window then opens on that level). Empty source follows the existing lastTabClosed path. (c) dropEvent: verified arora mime takes precedence over the legacy url drop — source==target is the old reorder, cross-widget is moveTabTo at insertIndexForDrop (midpoint split; header band appends). (d) moveTabTo refuses aroraNoTabTransfer pages (future engine hosts) and persistent pages while the process is private/Tor — OTR itself is per-page so a private tab stays private wherever it lands; container binding rides on the WebView and obeys the target's two-level filter (merged container tabs file into their level's hidden store when it isn't active — the strip stays on its current header, documented CONT06 rule). WebView/LocationBar dragEnter/drop ignore the arora mime so a tab drag can't hijack into a navigation. Verify: tst_TabWidget::tabTransfer (move+identity+pin/group-remap/custom-title legs, live merge drop through testDropEvent with zero loadUrl emits, stale-payload fallback, OTR/private guard, real BrowserMainWindow tear-off) 3/3; tst_tabwidget 22/0; make check green except the 2 documented clean-HEAD flakes (torChromeTheme, colorSchemeApply).

### READ03

Reader mode intermittently shows only the page title, no article text — user report: 'sometimes it shows only the titles of the page not the text.' reader.js enter() guards !article || !article.content (~161) but a non-empty-yet-thin content passes, and the home-grown sanitize() (~227) may strip nearly all body nodes on some pages (content hidden inside iframes/picture/figure/section wrappers it removes). Spec: (a) DIAGNOSE FIRST — reproduce on a corpus of real article pages (news, blog, docs; the QTcpServer tst fixture pattern can serve captured html); log what parse() returns vs what sanitize() leaves vs what renders — pin down whether the loss is Readability-thin-content, sanitizer-over-strip, or a doc-mutation race between probe() and cloneNode(true).parse(); (b) THIN-CONTENT GUARD — if parse() returns content under a sane floor (e.g. textContent length or paragraph count below Readability's own charThreshold ~500), treat as extraction failure NOT a renderable article — enter() already returns {ok:false} for null content; extend the same to degenerate content so the button stays available but doesn't put up a header-only overlay (or shows an honest 'reader unavailable for this page' state — pick one, document); (c) SANITIZER — if sanitize() is the culprit, fix the allowlist so real body markup survives (it must keep stripping interactive/dangerous nodes — the security intent stays); (d) keep READ01/READ02 behavior — probe gating, tor no-channel path, font/theme prefs, exit/restore-scroll. Verify: a corpus page that previously rendered title-only now either renders full text or refuses honestly; sanitizer regression leg (dangerous markup still stripped); tst readerMode leg on a thin-content fixture; make check PASS.

Done note (2026-10-13): the QTcpServer-fixture corpus surfaced THREE distinct loss paths. (1) Sanitizer over-strip — the e2e diagnostic printed parse=1848 oldSan=0 on a <form>-wrapped article: Readability's conditional cleaner keeps a text-heavy <form>, then the old blanket removal deleted the node WITH all its children — the literal "title but no body" report. sanitize() now unwraps form+noscript as transparent containers (children spliced into the parent, wrapper dropped; noscript is unwrapped not kept because scripting is on in the live document) and the dead-list keeps script/iframe/object/embed/applet/input/button/select/textarea/link/meta/base/template plus the on*-attribute and javascript:-href scrub. (2) Embed-clone wedge — document.cloneNode(true) on a page containing object/embed/applet/iframe/frame/frameset made Blink tear down/rebuild plugin widgets in the detached document and the renderer world died asynchronously (probe/enter callbacks never arrived — "intermittent"); these are now stripped from the clone before parse() — Readability removes them from article content anyway. (3) Degenerate extraction — Readability's longest-under-threshold-attempt fallback can return heading-only content; enter() now sanitizes before building the overlay and refuses with {ok:false} when the sanitized body is under MIN_ARTICLE_CHARS (500, matching charThreshold) with no img/video/audio/svg/table/figure — the existing ReaderMode::message path surfaces "Reader view is not available on this page." on the status bar (the honest-refusal state per (b)). Verify: new data-driven tst_BrowserMainWindow::readerModeContent — /form (parse=1848 oldSan=0 in the diagnostic log; renders "Reader corpus paragraph", no <form> survives), /thin (aside-body article; enter() refuses, message emitted, no overlay), /danger (text renders; <script, onerror, javascript: all stripped — fixture payloads write a flag, not alert(), because a modal JS alert pauses the renderer's world and would wedge the test before the assert) — 3/3 PASS offscreen QTWEBENGINE_CHROMIUM_FLAGS=--disable-gpu; make check result in Notes.

### READ05

Reader mode security hardening — user constraint: 'I want the reader mode as it works today from user point of view — click it and the page turns into reader mode.' Spec: keep the user-facing behavior IDENTICAL (button toggles reader, clean article overlay, font-size controls, light/dark theme, exit restores original DOM+scroll — all READ01/READ02/READ03 behavior preserved); harden the pipeline underneath: (a) ISOLATED EXTRACTION — stop injecting reader.js/Readability into the page's main world; run probe+parse in a non-zero QWebEngineScript worldId (see engineinterface.h ~195 Engine::Script::worldId) OR an offscreen worker page fed the serialized DOM — page JS must not be able to see/redefine __aroraReader/Readability; (b) SANITIZE IN C++ — extract() returns the article HTML fragment, sanitize it app-side in C++ (allowlist — port the reader.js sanitize() policy to C++ where page JS cannot tamper with it) before anything re-enters a document; (c) SANDBOXED-IFRAME OVERLAY — render the article in a full-viewport iframe with sandbox attrs (allow-same-origin only what the font/theme controls need; no allow-scripts for article content — the chrome controls can live outside or use permitted channels) rather than innerHTML'd shadow DOM inside the live document — shadow DOM is style-encapsulation not a security boundary; iframe must look identical (same CSS, same controls, same scroll). Keep: tor no-channel fallback, JSCTL-lift still working (or better: worker-page approach removes the need to lift JS on blocked pages — preferred if feasible, document), __aroraChannel bootstrap only in the isolated world. Verify: identical UX checklist (enter/exit/scroll-restore/font/theme/probe gating/tor window), hostile fixture pages (script/event-handler/javascript: URL/mutation-XSS attempts inside article markup) render inert, page-side tampering with Readability/__aroraReader no longer affects extraction, sanitizer bypass attempts neutralized C++-side, tst legs + READ03's corpus fixtures still pass, make check PASS. NOTE: the shared-channel split (external/aroraAutofill/arora/aroraReader on one main-world channel) is tracked separately as SEC24 on master — do NOT fold it in here.

Done note (2026-10-13): implemented as specced, UX unchanged. (a) ReaderWorldId = QWebEngineScript::UserWorld+100 in readermode.cpp — the whole scriptBundle (Readability-readerable + Readability + reader.js) evaluates there via WebEnginePageAdapter::runJavaScriptLiftedOn's new worldId param (main world still the default; the JSCTL lift path forwards the id too), so page JS cannot see or redefine __aroraReader/Readability/isProbablyReaderable while DOM access is unchanged. (b) reader.js's old DOMParser sanitize() is deleted; extract() returns the raw fragment and ReaderMode::enter() passes it through the new C++ ReaderSanitizer (src/readersanitizer.{h,cpp} — a real tokenizer, not regex: script/style/iframe/object/embed/input/button/select/template/link/meta/base drops, form+noscript transparent unwrap keeping READ03's fix, on*/ping/srcdoc attribute scrub, entity-decoded + whitespace-squashed javascript:/vbscript:/data: URL filtering, foreign-content-aware /> emission). (c) The overlay is still a fixed Shadow-DOM host (scroll/exit behavior identical) but the article now renders in a full-viewport sandboxed srcdoc iframe — sandbox="allow-same-origin allow-top-navigation-by-user-activation allow-popups allow-popups-to-escape-sandbox", no allow-scripts — so nothing in the content executes even if the sanitizer missed it; links still behave like the old overlay. Chrome controls live in the shadow root and report via "arora:reader:*" CustomEvents on the shared document — reader-forward.js (a DocumentCreation main-world QWebEngineScript) relays them to the existing aroraReader channel object; the bridge's setPreference stays key+value-clamped so forged events gain nothing, and tor pages (no channel, no forwarder) keep the working overlay minus persistence. READ03's thin-content floor moved into the pipeline (sanitized textLength < 500 with no media refuses via the status-bar message). SEC24 channel split untouched as required. Verify: new tst readerSanitizer (wrapper unwrap, dangerous-tag/attr/URL legs incl. entity+numeric+whitespace-encoded javascript:, safe link/img preservation, text-length/media accounting) + readerModeContent /form /thin /danger /tamper (page redefines __aroraReader+Readability — extraction unaffected) /hostile (payloads inert, injected script/handler in iframe blocked — Qt logs the expected sandbox denial) + readerModeButton 9/9 PASS; make check: check-warnings PASS, all suites green except fileSchemeErrors (documented flake — PASS standalone) offscreen --disable-gpu.
