### ICONS06

Tor window nav-button icons unreadable on the violet chrome — user screenshot: back/forward/reload/home/app-menu-logo render pale/washed; 'change it like the reader mode or onion button color.' Root cause: the tor stylesheet lifts QToolButton text color to #eee (browsermainwindow.cpp ~150) and reader/circuit buttons paint their glyph FROM the palette so the ButtonText->#eee lift (~283-290) reaches them — but the standard nav buttons use AroraIcon::get() themed QIcons whose artwork keeps its own colors; a stylesheet can't recolor QIcon pixmaps. Spec: (a) give the nav buttons the same treatment as reader/onion — either tint their icon pixmaps to #eee in tor mode (AroraIcon could expose a tor/light variant — check if ICONS04's family work already has a recolor path to reuse) or repaint them palette-driven like ReaderButton does; app-menu logo on tor: the arora logo png stays colored (brand mark) but check its legibility on violet — if it fades, add a subtle light backing/outline variant, document choice; (b) cover EVERY nav-bar widget — back/forward/reload/home/stop-reload/reader/piP/sidebar-toggle/app-menu/etc — audit the whole row, not just the visible offender; the tab strip, menu text and status bar are already handled by TOR07/11/14 — buttons only here; (c) non-tor windows unchanged (dark-theme check too — a dark non-tor theme may have the same wash-out, worth a look while there). Verify: offscreen/real grab on tor window — every nav glyph legible at #eee-ish contrast vs before, normal window unchanged, MENU07's bigger logo still legible on violet, tst appMenuButton/nav legs, make check PASS.

Done: new AroraIcon::tinted(icon,color,disabledColor)/getTinted(name,...) — resolves the base art through getOnDark then SourceIn-recolors every opaque pixel flat (alpha preserved) at sizes 16-48, seeding a deliberate Disabled pixmap instead of Qt's washed-out generated ghosting; theme-independent so the "native" pick (no -dark art, the reported path) lifts too. Tor block: back/forward/reload/stop/home -> getTinted(#eee,#b9aecf), sidebar toggle -> tinted(SidebarPanel::icon), app-menu button -> torAppMenuIcon() (colored bear over a translucent #eee disc — brand mark kept, silhouette readable on violet) + ButtonText lift for the menu-indicator arrow, ZoomControl::useOnDarkIcons -> getTinted (same violet status bar). Audit: nav row = appMenuButton, back, forward, stopReload, home, reader+onion (already palette-painted #eee), location-bar field (light surface — untouched), sidebar toggle, Tor badge — all covered; tab strip/menus/status-bar text stay with TOR07/11/14; non-tor windows untouched (dark-palette path already covered by ICONS05's -dark/dark-aware resolution, re-verified by darkChromeIcons).

### TOR16

Tor circuit panel defects — user report: 'the circuit for this site button is not big enough, the connection chain is not viewable just the flags, but not correct. Not stable enough — sometimes it is big enough but the chain lines are not viewable.' Spec: (a) CHAIN VISIBILITY — TorChainView paints dots on a connector + flag + title + detail lines per hop (torcircuitpanel.cpp ~44-150); user sees ONLY flags, no connector lines/text — diagnose: detail lines clipped by row height? painted in wrong color on violet? node.details empty so nothing renders? connector drawLine needs >=2 centers — single/empty hop case draws nothing, plus flag correctness vs geoip country code lookup (flagIcon ~128 — wrong flags reported); (b) SIZE STABILITY — 'sometimes big enough, sometimes not' smells like the popup sizes BEFORE circuit data lands and never re-lays-out (TOR10's no-relayout rule may have over-pinned) — the panel must size to the FULL chain (recompute sizeHint when nodes arrive; the popup may need a settle-relayout or a fixed min-size that fits N hops); also make the 'Circuit for this site' trigger button adequately sized if it renders cramped on the violet bar; (c) verify the chain visually matches TOR15's reference (This browser -> Guard (flag+country+IP+fp) -> Middle -> Exit -> site) — all connector segments + hop rows visible every open, not intermittently. Verify: repeated open/close on a live tor circuit shows the full painted chain every time (multi-hop, correct flags vs codes), single-hop/loading/dead states render honestly not blank, button sized like siblings, tor tst legs + grab reviewed, make check PASS.

Done: root cause was stale popup geometry, not paint/palette — a shown QMenu hosting a QWidgetAction never re-lays-out when the hosted widget's sizeHint grows, and QMenu::sizeHint() caches the open-time footprint outright; clicking before the circuit snapshot landed opened the popup at the info-state size (~222x132) and the 350x306 chain stayed squeezed into a ~24px-tall sliver showing only flag fragments. Reproduced with a faithful harness (real TorCircuitButton/Panel/Manager + faketor, xcb): menu opened 222x132, panel hint grew to 350x306, menu stayed put. Fixes: (a) TorCircuitButton::growMenuToFit() — resizes a visible menu to panel->sizeHint()+chrome via expandedTo (grow-only, never shrinks — preserves TOR13's no-creep since signature-gated refreshes touch nothing); called from applyPanelContent/syncOpenMenu/onNewCircuit + NEWNYM status updates; (b) TorChainView::sizeHint() reserves the standard 5-node chain (browser + 3 hops + site) so the size is stable across rotations; (c) connector pen switched from palette Mid to translucent WindowText (Mid can equal the window color on flat/high-contrast themes); (d) text rects clamped so a squeezed widget never hands drawText a negative width; (e) onion button pinned to a 20px iconSize rendered from a 4x source pixmap, New-circuit button min-height 30px. Country codes already uppercased + "??" discarded in tormanager — flags were correct. New tst leg circuitPanelGrowsToFit (faketor: open small, chain lands, menu grows, chain widget fits its hint, New circuit works). Verified: tst_tormanager 17/0/1skip, --tor-circuit-click-smoke PASS 60 open/close cycles no crash, main build clean, chain grab shows full connector+dots+flags+titles+details.

### TOR17

Tor window unusable — every request net::ERR_PROXY_CONNECTION_FAILED incl URL-bar searches — user: 'make it workable by default as like everywhere.' Diagnosis so far: URL-bar search RESOLVES correctly (DDG url built — SRCH path fine); the profile's SOCKS proxy IS configured and attempted, but NO tor daemon ever spawns: binary exists at ~/.local/share/Arora/Arora/tor/tor (fetch-tor.sh bundle, runs standalone), yet no DataDirectory, no control-port file, no tor process, no TorManager log lines — TorManager::start() (browserapplication.cpp ~311) either never fires or resolveBinary() fails inside the app's sandbox (bwrap read-blocks may hide ~/.local/share paths — check whether the sandboxed process sees AppDataLocation/tor, and whether QProcess can exec children under the policy). Spec: (a) find why start/resolve fails in the real sandboxed session — instrument/fix so the bundled binary is found + spawned reliably (sandbox policy fix if needed — tor must be spawnable, its DataDirectory writable); (b) DEFAULT-WORKABLE — a tor window should self-heal: if tor can't launch, show the honest tor state (circuit panel already has a dead-state UI) + surface 'tor daemon unavailable' clearly instead of every tab dying at a raw proxy error — and NEVER silently fall back to clearnet (that's deanonymization); if the bundle is missing entirely, point at BuildProcess/fetch-tor.sh or trigger it (consent: it's a network fetch — document the choice); (c) URL search and normal browsing must then work through the tor proxy like everywhere else. Verify: tor window loads pages through the running daemon (control-port + DataDirectory appear, bootstrap reaches 100%), failure states are honest not ERR_PROXY_CONNECTION_FAILED-everywhere, no clearnet leak on daemon failure, make check PASS.

Outcome (2026-10-13): the spawn path itself proved sound in-process — the fragility is environmental: the expert-bundle tor has no RPATH, so an inherited loader path (bundle wrapper / sandbox runtime) can bind incompatible libssl/libevent and kill the child silently, and any pre-bootstrap exit instantly declared failure. Fixes: (a) TorManager prepends the resolved binary's dir to the child's LD_LIBRARY_PATH (DYLD_FALLBACK_LIBRARY_PATH on macOS) so shipped libs win; (b) bounded self-heal — early exit/launch error respawns up to 2x at 1.5s intervals (retryOrFail), cancelled by stop(), budget reset on Ready / explicit restart; (c) new TorStatusBanner above the tabs — shows Connecting/bootstrap % while starting and 'Tor daemon unavailable — <reason>' + Retry on failure, hidden when Ready — replaces raw ERR_PROXY_CONNECTION_FAILED mystery; proxy stays fail-closed 127.0.0.1:1 throughout, no clearnet path; (d) tor stderr notice lines now reach qWarning and a failed startDetached() warns. Verified: tst_tormanager 21/21 incl. new retryOnEarlyExit/retryRecovers/statusBanner legs + realDaemonLifecycle (real 0.4.9.13 bootstrap), --tor-window-smoke PASS (banner present 'Connecting to the Tor network…', socks routed, NAM+WebEngine IsTor:true, live 3-hop circuit), --tor-smoke PASS (bootstrap 100%, socks ready, daemon reaped), make check: check-warnings PASS, 65 programs 0 failed after rebuilding a stale settings.o left by a non-rustdl qmake regen (downloadsPage PASS standalone 45/45) — offscreen --disable-gpu, ARORA_TOR_BINARY=~/.local/share/Arora/Arora/tor/tor for test-mode AppDataLocation.

### ICONS07

Menu entries missing icons — user: 'not every function have icon within the menus.' The bundled sets ship only 33 freedesktop names, so 19 new mid-grey (#7a7a7a) glyphs were authored in `src/icons/arora-legacy/icons/` — SHLD01's convention: every bundled theme resolves them through `Inherits=arora-legacy`, native mode substitutes real desktop artwork where the name is standard (go-up/go-down, go-jump, document-import/-export, preferences-system, text-x-generic, text-html, view-icon, view-history, utilities-terminal) and falls back to the legacy art otherwise; custom names (view-menubar, view-toolbar, view-tabbar, view-statusbar, zoom-text, view-pip, view-private, tor-onion) come out of the bundle everywhere. Wiring: File (private-tab mask, tor onion, go-jump, import/export, preferences), Edit (find next/prev arrows), View (all five bar toggles + icon-only/zoom-text/reader/pip/source + both DevTools children), History tail (Show All History view-history, Clear History edit-clear in history.cpp postPopulated), Bookmarks (Bookmark All Tabs bookmark-new; the 'Open in Tabs' tail in folder flyouts gets tab-new), Window (Search Tabs edit-find), Tools (Command Palette system-run, Clear Private Data edit-clear, Lock Credential Store security-high), File > New Container Tab statics (grey colorIcon chip for No Container, list-add, preferences-system) so they sit in column with the color-chip container rows. Documented exceptions held: separators, submenu headers (asserted icon-free), and dynamic rows (history/bookmark/site entries, window list, encodings, user agents, container chips have their own). Tor windows run the same menus on the platform palette (TOR07) so no special tinting was needed. Verified: new tst_BrowserMainWindow::menuIcons (post-popup audit — File/Edit/View/Tools/Help assert zero iconless leafs + non-null pixmaps, container/devtools submenu leafs, required-text checks on History/Bookmarks/Window, tor-window File menu leg) 55/55; tst_aroraicon 12/12, tst_historyui 8/8, tst_modelmenu 11/11; ARORA_MENU_GRAB_DIR File+View grabs reviewed — uniform icon column, legible glyphs; make check: check-warnings PASS, all programs green except the known clean-HEAD flake fileSchemeErrors (PASS standalone, 212ms) offscreen --disable-gpu.

### EXT08

Extension action buttons never surface — user report: 'when I turned on the bitwarden the extension's icon is not viewable anywhere. it should be viewed after the engine button.' Root cause confirmed: BrowserMainWindow never references ExtensionManager — `ExtensionInfo::actionPopupUrl` (extensionmanager.h:72, populated in extensionmanager.cpp:69) is captured but no chrome hosts it, so a loaded extension with an action popup is invisible. Spec: (a) NAV-BAR HOST — a container widget in m_navigationSplitter immediately AFTER m_engineIndicatorHost (right-adjacent to the engine button per the user's ask; keep engine button position) holding one QToolButton per enabled+loaded extension whose actionPopupUrl is non-empty; zero buttons => host hidden, no gap. (b) BUTTONS — icon from the manifest's action.default_icon or best-fit icons[] entry loaded from the extension's `path` (scale to nav iconSize; small pixmap cache keyed by extension id); fallback = a generic puzzle/extension glyph via AroraIcon; tooltip = extension name; click opens the popup. (c) POPUP — a Qt.Popup-framed widget hosting a QWebEngineView bound to the extension's own profile loading actionPopupUrl — Chrome-style anchored dropdown, sensible default size (~360x500) that follows the popup document's sizeHint where feasible, closes on focus-out/escape; NEVER opens as a normal tab. (d) LIFECYCLE — rebuild the set on extensionLoaded/extensionUnloaded + install/uninstall + enable/disable transitions (signals exist at extensionmanager.h:204-207); per-window buttons track that window's profile — OTR/private and tor profiles never load extensions (EXT05 exclusion) so no buttons appear there; multiple windows each get their own host. (e) Bitwarden is the reference case: with Bitwarden enabled, its glyph shows right of the engine indicator and clicking opens its real popup UI. Keep SEC24 channel split intact — the popup QWebEngineView is a fresh view, wire it like any other profile page. Verify: enable Bitwarden -> button right of engine indicator on a normal window; click -> popup opens and is functional; disable/unload -> button gone; second window mirrors; tor + private windows show none; grabs reviewed (normal + tor); new tst legs (host button count tracks popup-bearing extensions, hidden-when-empty, popup opens on click, no host on OTR/tor); make check PASS.

Outcome (2026-10-13): new `ExtensionActionBar` (src/extensions/extensionactionbar.{h,cpp}) — a flat strip of QToolButtons in `m_navigationSplitter` immediately after the location-bar stack (splitter index 1). Note on placement: this branch predates the engine-switcher — `m_engineIndicatorHost` exists on master (ENG05/ENG08) but not in this tree, so the bar holds the cell the indicator will occupy and a code comment flags it for the merge (bar moves to the cell right after the indicator at merge time — the user's 'after the engine button' intent). One button per extension that is enabled + loaded + has a non-empty `actionPopupUrl`; zero matches => `setVisible(false)` so the splitter shows no gap (`setCollapsible(1,false)` + zero stretch keeps it invisible-but-present). Icons resolve from the extension's own manifest at `info.path`: `action`/`browser_action`/`page_action`.default_icon first (string or size map), else best-fit top-level `icons[]` (smallest >= target, else largest), scaled to the nav iconSize and cached in a bounded per-id QHash; a new mid-grey `application-x-addon` puzzle glyph in arora-legacy is the fallback via `AroraIcon::get` (native themes substitute real artwork). Tooltip + accessibleName = extension name. Click opens a `Qt::Popup`-flagged QFrame (objectName `extensionActionPopup`) anchored under the button holding a fresh `QWebEngineView` + plain `QWebEnginePage` on `BrowserApplication::webEngineProfile()` — deliberately not the app's WebPage subclass, so no arora QWebChannel objects or page policy leak into extension chrome (SEC24 split kept); it never goes through the tab path. Default 360x500, post-load the document's scroll size re-fits the bubble (clamped 280-800 x 120-600) and re-anchors; Escape (event filter) / focus-out (Qt::Popup) / re-clicking the serving button all close it; `WA_DeleteOnClose` + parenting to the bar fixed a teardown crash where a parentless popup's page outlived the profile. Rebuild rides all five manager signals (loaded/unloaded/installed/uninstalled/changed — the enable/disable pulse). Private and tor windows never construct the host at all (guarded by `BrowserApplication::isPrivate()` — their profiles may not load extensions). Verified: new `tst_BrowserMainWindow::extensionActionButtons` (MV3 fixture via QTemporaryDir, splitter index, hidden-when-empty, icon/tooltip/objectName/count legs, popup Qt::Popup flag + chrome-extension url + profile identity + zero chrome-extension tabs, re-click toggle close, second-window mirror, disable-removes leg, private + tor windows carry no bar, `ARORA_EXT_GRAB_DIR` nav grab reviewed — button visible right of the URL bar) 3/3 PASS x3 runs; full tst_browsermainwindow 56/56 on a quiet machine; check-warnings PASS; `make check` all programs green except documented clean-HEAD flakes — the `torChromeTheme` flake leaves `s_torMode` latched and cascaded 13 legs under rust-lane build load (~30s contention timeouts vs 20s limits) plus `tst_tormanager::circuitMenuNoRequeryLoop` — every failed leg PASSes standalone, including mine (TABS12 documented the identical cascade on clean HEAD).

### EXT09

Extension version not shown — user request: 'add the version number of the extension at the preferences-extension.' `ExtensionInfo::version` is already parsed from the manifest (extensionmanager.h:87, populated at :296) but `refreshExtensionList` (settings.cpp ~2886) only renders Name/Status/Type columns — ui-work predates EXT06's Description column on master, so handle WHICHEVER column set is present at merge time (EXT06 may merge in first — put Version after Name or beside Description per the existing column order, column 0 stays the checkable name column). Verify: Bitwarden row shows its real version; built-ins show version or honest blank; a fake update fixture shows the available-version surface; column order sane in a grab; tst legs for the new column if the tree is asserted; make check PASS.

Outcome (2026-10-13): `extensionsTree` gained a Version column between
Name and Status — `QWebEngineExtensionInfo` exposes no version
accessor in Qt 6.12, so the cell is read at render time via
`ExtensionManager::inspectManifest(info.path).version` (the same call
the details pane already makes); path-less built-ins render an honest
"—".  A finished update check surfaces in-cell as "7.7.7 → 9.9.9
available" (picked over a tooltip for glanceability — the rich detail
still lives in the details label); `extensionUpdateCheckFinished` now
calls `refreshExtensions()` so landed `updateResultFor` offers repaint
(the refresh already ends in `extensionSelectionChanged`).  Status and
Source moved to columns 2/3 — the only column-indexed writes were in
`refreshExtensions`; `extensionItemChanged`/`removeExtension`/
`extensionSelectionChanged` all key on column 0 / `Qt::UserRole` and
were unaffected.  Test gotcha worth knowing: `saveAndReload` persists
`proxy/enabled=true` (localhost:8080) and the shared
`NetworkAccessManager` caches it in its proxy factory, so the gupdate
fetch dies in the full suite but passes standalone —
`extensionVersionColumn` clears `proxy/enabled` + calls
`NetworkAccessManager::instance()->loadSettings()` for the leg and
restores it after.

### TABS17

Vertical rail rows too short — text clipped — user report with grab: 'the tab height is not good. the history is still trunketed' — the History row's text is visibly cut at the row's bottom edge while neighbors look fine. The vertical rail paints via TABS02's forced-North rows; row height comes from QTabBar's tabSizeHint influenced by TABS07's metrics bound and RSZ02's persisted strip height (horizontalTabHeight, clamp 20-64, tabbar.cpp ~2117-2213). Suspects: (a) a persisted/explicit strip height below the text floor pins vertical rows too — the floor should be fontMetrics().height() + padding for text-bearing rows, never less; (b) the custom row paint's icon/text centering math (-2/-3 offsets at ~1586/1597) may mis-center the text baseline so glyphs clip even when the rect is adequate; (c) a row-level per-tab height variance (history rows with the clock close-widget?) leaves the text box shorter than siblings. Spec: (a) reproduce with a grab of a vertical rail hosting History/pinned/renamed rows and identify whether rect height or text placement is wrong; (b) enforce a text floor — vertical rows never render shorter than fontMetrics height + the same vertical padding the horizontal Automatic path uses; user-set heights below it clamp at paint/layout time, not into unreadability; (c) fix the baseline centering so elided text stays inside its rect top and bottom; (d) both rails (left/right), tor violet + dark themes, icon-only rows unaffected. Verify: grab shows every row's title fully painted with symmetric padding, History row no longer clipped, horizontal strips unchanged, tst_tabbar row legs + make check PASS.

### TOR18

Tor-window loads still die during bootstrap — user: 'tor url search is not working. make it default.' Verified on the live session: the daemon spawns and reaches BOOTSTRAP 100% (TOR17 fix works — SOCKS answers, IsTor:true end-to-end), but every navigation issued during the ~1-2min warmup window fails permanently with net::ERR_PROXY_CONNECTION_FAILED / stream timeouts and the error page just sits there — the user must manually reload to ever get a page. Requests right after ready still race cold exit circuits for a while (tor log: 'tried connecting ... giving up' bursts just after 100%). Spec: (a) URL-bar searches and navigations issued while !TorManager::isReady() are QUEUED and dispatched when `ready` fires instead of erroring — at minimum the current tab and any user-kicked load during the banner's Connecting state; (b) loads that land on an error page while tor is Connecting or inside a short post-ready warm-up (~30-60s, circuits still cold) auto-reload once `ready`/first-circuit fires — bounded (one auto-retry per navigation, no loop); TOR17's banner already communicates state, keep it; (c) daemon failure outright keeps the honest error (Retry via banner); (d) search dispatch already resolves engines correctly — don't regress engineForContext's private-engine pick. Verify: fresh tor window + immediate query loads by itself once circuits are up — no manual reload; a mid-warmup second query also lands; killed daemon still fails honestly; tst legs for queued-navigation-until-ready if testable; make check PASS.

Done (2026-10-14): (a) WebView::loadUrl holds the newest remote url (http/https/ws/wss/ftp) while !TorManager::isReady() — local schemes never queue, the wake-up is one lazy `ready` connect per view (+destroyed re-arm), the flush re-enters loadUrl so a still-unready daemon re-queues rather than hitting the fail-closed proxy; every funnel (URL bar/search/click/drop/bookmark/history) routes through loadUrl, engine pick untouched (d). (b) WebPage arms a bounded retry for genuine main-frame failures in Starting/Connecting/Bootstrapping or inside a 45s post-ready warm-up (new TorManager::readyElapsed clock); bound spent on arm (m_torRetriedUrl) so it can never loop; disarm on the next accepted real navigation with the data: carve-out for the error page's own setHtml commit; fires on first BUILT circuit at >=3s dwell — the observed bootstrap BUILT reports arrive before exit paths stabilize — else the 15s ready-fallback timer. (c) Failed/Stopped/Stopping never arms — the error page + TOR17 banner Retry stay honest. New --tor-window-smoke leg drives a queued nav on a background tab and gates PASS on real check.torproject.org content committing by itself. Verified live against a real daemon: held during bootstrap -> flushed at ready -> first attempt died on a cold circuit -> error page -> retry re-dispatched and committed 'Congratulations. This browser is configured to use Tor.' — zero manual reloads. Tests: tst_WebView::torQueuedNavigation (queue/replace/local-bypass/flush/Failed-holds), tst_TorManager::readyElapsed. tst_webview 24/24, tst_tormanager 21/21, tst_webpage 73/73, check-warnings PASS, runTests 65+ programs 0 failed offscreen --disable-gpu. ENVIRONMENT REPAIR: a plain `qmake` regen of autotests/webview (missing CONFIG+=rustdl) had rebuilt ~43 shared .obj objects without ARORA_RUSTDL, breaking tst_settingsdialog::downloadsPage and (via mixed-config objects) flaking tst_browsermainwindow — deleted the stale objects, rebuilt under `qmake CONFIG+=rustdl`, all green.

### TOR19

Tor circuit popover needs two clicks — user: 'I need to click 2 times on the tor icon to be able to see the pop over window correctly.' The popup is a QMenu hosting TorCircuitPanel via QWidgetAction (torcircuitbutton.cpp ~291-313) — the classic first-show race: on click 1 the menu sizes BEFORE the panel's content lays out (empty/squashed popup — this is exactly the 'intermittently too small, chain lines missing' shape TOR16 described but evidently didn't fully kill), click 2 the menu reuses the now-laid-out panel and looks right. Spec: (a) reproduce — grab the popup on FIRST click after a fresh tor window (not after a warmup open) and confirm the wrong-size/wrong-content state; (b) fix the first-show path — candidates: the aboutToShow connect at :316 runs refresh but the menu's sizeHint is computed before the panel's layout settles — force an updateGeometry/ensurePolished + resize on the menu before it maps, or pre-lay-out the panel once at construct time so its sizeHint is real on first popup; check whether a stale minimum size from the first wrong show persists and re-show uses it; (c) the fix must survive circuit-data arriving ASYNC — first click while circuit data is still loading should show the correct-size popup with a loading/empty state, not a collapsed one, then fill in — TOR16's 'no re-layout after open' rule should not mean 'no first-layout either'; (d) no flicker/regression on repeat opens — subsequent clicks stay instant and correctly sized. Verify: first-click grab on a fresh tor window shows the full-size chain popover (not collapsed), second click identical, popup during still-connecting state sized sanely, tst legs for first-show geometry if scriptable (the button already has panelContentSignature/test seams); make check PASS.

Done (2026-10-14): root cause traced through Qt 6.12 internals — QToolButtonPrivate::showMenu() (InstantPopup) calls menu->sizeHint() BEFORE popup() to pick the initial position; that flush runs QMenuPrivate::updateActionRects() which measures the QWidgetAction from the panel's CURRENT content and clears itemsDirty; QMenuPrivate::popup() then emits aboutToShow (our populateMenu) but the post-populate updateActionRects early-returns because no item changed — the menu opens at the stale rect and only TOR16's growMenuToFit can patch it (grow-only, never shrinks, and async data keeps it wrong on some paths). Click 2 works because the panel still holds the previous populate's render. Fix (torcircuitbutton): (a) panel stays synced while the menu is closed — ctor calls applyPanelContent() after wiring, and syncOpenMenu()'s closed branch refreshes content instead of early-returning, so the pre-popup flush measures real content; (b) every populateMenu() toggles m_panelAction visible false->true — the ActionChanged event marks itemsDirty so the post-aboutToShow pass always recomputes the action rect from the fresh panel hint (one extra rect pass per open, negligible); (c) TOR13's signature gate + TOR16's growMenuToFit unchanged — no requery loops, no creep, visible growth still grow-only. Verified: new tst_BrowserMainWindow::torCircuitPopupFirstShow (pre-popup sizeHint flush, width growth, mid-open NEWNYM growth, status-clear shrink, repeat open — fails 383x130-vs-383x110 on pre-fix code); tst_tormanager::circuitMenuNoRequeryLoop gained a pre-popup flush + panel-size==sizeHint first-show leg; --tor-circuit-click-smoke PASS 60 cycles under faketor with new mid-open probes that clickFail if the shown panel doesn't match its live sizeHint on first-open and first-ready-open; ARORA_TOR_GRAB_DIR grabs reviewed — full 3-hop chain on first click after Ready, sane connecting-state popup before it; make check rc=0 (check-warnings PASS, 67 programs 0 failed) offscreen --disable-gpu.

### PKG01

Linux packaging 1/3 — Debian .deb (user: 'I need deb, flatpak, rpm (Suse), Windows 10/11, macOS DMG'). Build a real .deb from the qmake build: (a) debian/ packaging metadata (control/rules/compat/copyright — check for existing skeleton first, AUD01 deleted an old builddeb.sh that referenced a removed debian-upstream/ — rebuild properly, don't resurrect the stale one); correct deps (Qt6 libs, libqt6webengine*, the rust .so artifacts libservo_embed + rustcore/rustdl must ship inside the package or link statically — decide + document); (b) desktop file + icon install (hicolor sizes), /usr/bin + /usr/lib layout per FHS, install.pri updated if the install rules are stale; (c) build via dpkg-buildpackage or a .devin/pkg-deb.sh script — reproducible, documented in README; (d) verify the package INSTALLS and runs: dpkg -i on this host (or container if cleaner), binary launches, finds its .so artifacts + Qt plugins, no missing-dep errors (ldd clean inside the package layout). Verify: .deb builds reproducibly, installs, launches; check-bundle.sh extended to cover the artifact if it fits; notes committed.

Done (2026-10-14): new `debian/` (control, changelog, rules, copyright, compat, source/format 3.0-native) — `rules` is a hand-rolled makefile delegating to `.devin/pkg-deb.sh` since debhelper is not installed and the tree builds against a user-local Qt 6.12. Artifact policy: the rust crates link STATICALLY via their `.a` archives (`CONFIG+=rustdl`/`rustcore` — the .pri's link `.a`, not the cdylib `.so`), so there are no rust .so artifacts to ship; libservo_embed does not exist yet (ENG03 gates it). The deb bundles the Qt/WebEngine runtime (same payload as bundle-linux.sh) under `/usr/lib/arora` — bin/, lib/, libexec/, plugins/, resources/, translations/ keep the bundle's qt.conf `Prefix=..` relative layout; `/usr/bin/arora` is a fixed-path launcher setting LD_LIBRARY_PATH; app data lives in `/usr/share/arora` with a `usr/lib/arora/share/arora` symlink so the bundle-first `installedDataDirectory()` lookup resolves without a PREFIX rebuild. Freedesktop: existing arora.desktop reused, hicolor 16/32/128/512 + scalable + xpm, man page gzipped, doc dir carries copyright/licenses/changelog.gz. `sqldrivers` plugins dropped (no QtSql usage; would only pull undeclarable mysql/pq/oci deps). Depends: dpkg-shlibdeps `--ignore-missing-info` over resolvable ELFs (in-package Qt resolution covers the Qt SONAMEs) + an ldd/dpkg -S scan for the files whose NEEDED set references host-absent libs — libxcb-cursor.so.0 → libxcb-cursor0 mapped explicitly (hard dep of libqxcb on X11, absent on this build host — correctly reported as the one missing dep at install). Verify (`pkg-deb.sh verify`, run by the `all` default and `make pkg-deb`): control sanity; real `dpkg --force-not-root --instdir --admindir -i` seeded with the host status db — unpacks fully, reaches `Status: install ok installed` via --force-depends for the one host-missing dep; ldd on the installed binary + QtWebEngineProcess inside bwrap with $QTDIR tmpfs-masked — 0 unresolved; `--quit-after-load` launches offscreen with a scrubbed env from the installed layout (wrapper prefix rebased into the scratch root — `dpkg -i` into / needs root; honest level: full payload + dpkg install path exercised, only the literal /usr prefix differs). Reproducible via `./.devin/pkg-deb.sh`, `make pkg-deb`, or canonical `dpkg-buildpackage -b -us -uc -rfakeroot -d` (artifacts in `../`). check-bundle.sh left untouched — the deb verify needs dpkg, so it lives in the driver; `make check-deb` is the gate. install.pri kept as-is: its PREFIX/DATADIR rules still serve system-Qt `make install` source builds — the deb deliberately uses bundled staging instead. Known impurity: the staged binary keeps its dev RUNPATH to ~/Qt (no patchelf installed) — harmless since the launcher's LD_LIBRARY_PATH precedes RUNPATH and nonexistent RPATHs are ignored.

### PKG02

Linux packaging 2/3 — Flatpak manifest. A flatpak manifest (org or io.arora.* id) that builds arora inside the KDE runtime/SDK: (a) manifest yaml — org.kde Platform/SDK matching our Qt 6 version, arora source module + the rust crates as additional sources (rust toolchain inside the SDK or the rust-extension — document the choice; servo-embed is heavy — check whether it's buildable in the sandbox or shipped as a prebuilt module, either is acceptable if documented); (b) sandbox permissions honest — network, x11/wayland, pulseaudio, filesystem access only what the browser needs; (c) flatpak-builder run locally produces an installable bundle; appdata/metainfo.xml for store listing. Verify: flatpak-builder completes, bundle installs + launches via flatpak run, engine + servo + tor paths smoke inside the sandbox (tor needs its bundled binary + ports — flag sandbox conflicts honestly); notes committed.

Done (2026-10-14): `io.arora.Arora.yaml` at the root + `flatpak/` (metainfo, `cargo-config.toml`, `cargo-sources.json`, `gen-cargo-sources.py`) + `.devin/pkg-flatpak.sh` (export / build / bundle / install / verify / clean; default export = `git ls-files` dirty tree, `--clean` = `git archive HEAD`) + `make pkg-flatpak`. Toolchain: `org.flatpak.Builder` installed `--user` supplies flatpak-builder (no host package needed); deps = `org.kde.Sdk`//6.11 + `io.qt.qtwebengine.BaseApp`//6.11 (QtWebEngine is not in org.kde.Platform — baseapp is the supported path; 6.11 is the newest Qt6 series flathub ships, matching Qt 6.12 in ABI terms) + `org.freedesktop.Sdk.Extension.rust-stable`//25.08. Rust: both crates build inside the SDK from `cargo-sources.json` — 191 lockfile crates vendored sha256-pinned with synthesized `.cargo-checksum.json` stubs (same shape flatpak-cargo-generator emits), `CARGO_NET_OFFLINE` enforced. Servo: libservo_embed does not exist yet (ENG03) — same policy as the deb. Tor: expert bundle 15.0.25 (sha256 `19cf1872…`, archive `strip-components: 0`) lands at `/app/lib/tor` + `/app/lib/data` = exactly what TorManager's `<bindir>/../lib/tor` + `binaryDir/../data` search expects; tor uses auto-assigned localhost ports (no fixed-port conflict); no host binary needed. finish-args = ipc, network, wayland+fallback-x11, dri, pulseaudio, cups, xdg-download + `QTWEBENGINEPROCESS_PATH`/`QTWEBENGINE_DICTIONARIES_PATH` — the Qt6 baseapp carries flatpak-aware renderer sandboxing so `QTWEBENGINE_DISABLE_SANDBOX` is NOT set; no session-bus, no host fs, no flatpak-spawn. Gotchas hit: archive sources default `strip-components: 1` (flattened the tor tarball), `.crate` needs `archive-type: tar-gzip`, vendored crates need the `.cargo-checksum.json` stub, flatpak-builder's exported `CFLAGS`/`CXXFLAGS`/`CPPFLAGS`/`LDFLAGS` get re-exported by make with qmake's values into the cargo recipe (aws-lc-sys jitterentropy demands -O0) — fixed in `rustdl.pri`/`rustcore.pri` with `env -u` (generic fix, also protects the deb/host path); `QTemporaryFile` for history saves moved next to the target (default /tmp → EXDEV rename inside flatpak = silent history loss); `QGuiApplication::setDesktopFileName(io.arora.Arora)` under `/.flatpak-info` for the Wayland app_id. Verify (`pkg-flatpak.sh verify`): manifest `--show-manifest` parses; `appstreamcli validate` clean; `flatpak build-bundle` → `dist/io.arora.Arora_0.2_x86_64.flatpak` (110M); `flatpak install --user` from the bundle file; inside the sandbox `ldd` clean, `/app/lib/tor/tor --version` runs (Tor 0.4.9.14), `--quit-after-load` PASS (QtWebEngine render up, no sandbox errors), `--tor --tor-window-smoke` PASS with real bootstrap (IsTor:true via check.torproject.org). Metainfo carries a `releases` stub; store screenshots intentionally omitted (no public URL to point at — a flathub submission would add them).

### PKG03

Linux packaging 3/3 — RPM for openSUSE. A .spec + rpmbuild flow producing an openSUSE-compatible RPM: (a) .spec file — BuildRequires Qt6 devel packages (opensuse package names — libqt6-qtwebengine-devel etc.), the rust toolchain buildrequires or vendored artifacts per the PKG01 decision (CONSISTENT with whatever the deb chose — same shipping policy for the .so artifacts); %files covering binary + libs + desktop file + icons; (b) .devin/pkg-rpm.sh or documented rpmbuild invocation — buildable locally even if this host isn't suse (mock/obs or a suse container — pick what's actually runnable here; if rpm tooling is unavailable locally, deliver the verified .spec + a container-based build command and mark verification level honestly); (c) deps + paths checked against opensuse conventions. Verify: spec passes rpmlint-level sanity, package builds (container if needed), installs + launches where verifiable; notes committed.

Done (2026-10-14): `rpm/arora.spec` + `.devin/pkg-rpm.sh` (build/stage/requires/verify/srctar/pack/container/clean) + `make pkg-rpm`. Artifact policy identical to PKG01 by construction — `pkg-rpm.sh stage` delegates to `pkg-deb.sh stage`, so deb and rpm can never drift (bundled Qt/WebEngine under /usr/lib/arora, /usr/bin launcher, static rust .a — nothing to ship, no servo). The spec disables `AutoReqProv` (the private runtime would corrupt dep scanning) and carries the payload's real NEEDED-minus-bundled SONAME surface as explicit capability `Requires:` (70 caps, interpreter excluded — the same surface dpkg-shlibdeps computed for the deb); `pkg-rpm.sh verify` recomputes it from the staged tree and fails on drift in BOTH directions. openSUSE conventions: `qt6-base/webengine/tools/qt5compat-devel` BuildRequires (verified against Tumbleweed package naming), `qt6-linguist` for TRANSLATIONS, `rust`+`cargo` for the static crates, `Release: 0`, `Group: Productivity/Networking/Web/Browsers`, `%suse_update_desktop_file` gated on `%{?suse_version}`, `Suggests: tor` (deb parity), `%license`/`%doc` tagging. `%{qtdir}` macro (env $QTDIR → ~/Qt/6.12.0/gcc_64 → `--define` override) selects the relocatable Qt the payload bundles. Verified on this host (no rpmbuild/rpm/mock/podman/docker — honestly the only unverifiable leg is the binary .rpm build itself): `stage` produced the real 386M payload, `verify` PASS — header/section sanity, every %files entry resolves, zero unowned staged files, Requires set == computed NEEDED scan; `srctar` produced a clean arora-0.2.tar.gz export; `pack` fails loudly with the exact openSUSE/tumbleweed container runbook (bind-mount repo + ~/Qt, zypper rpm-build, QTDIR env). An .rpm built that way gets the same masked-Qt ldd + offscreen `--quit-after-load` verify as the deb (verify_rpm leg).

### ICONS10

Default icon set is the OS 'native' theme — user: 'icons are not good, some look the same; use open-source icons as default.' Diagnosis: AroraIcon defaults to s_nativeId (aroraicon.cpp:40,317 — system icon theme via QIcon::fromTheme); any chrome name missing from the user's system theme falls back to a generic/duplicate glyph, which is exactly the 'some icons look the same' symptom. We already BUNDLE four open-source sets: arora-adwaita, arora-breeze, arora-tabler (+ -dark variants), arora-legacy (src/icons/, ~35 svgs each, imported via import-icons.sh — all open-source). Spec: (a) DEFAULT CHANGE — make a bundled set the default (pick Breeze or Tabler — tabler is the cleanest modern line set; decide against dark-variant completeness; keep 'native' selectable in the icon-theme combo for users who want OS look); (b) COVERAGE AUDIT — enumerate every chrome icon name the code requests (menus, nav bar, sidebar, prefs list, dialogs) vs the bundled set's contents: any name missing from the chosen set gets the icon added (import from the same upstream set via import-icons.sh — not a lookalike from a different set) or the call site re-pointed at a name that exists — no silent fallback duplicates; (c) same audit for the dark variant so dark palettes don't lose glyphs; (d) the prefs icon-theme preview combo keeps working; tor window icons verified under the dark variant. Verify: fresh profile (no iconTheme key) shows the bundled set everywhere; grab every menu + nav bar + prefs page list — zero duplicate-fallback glyphs; dark + tor grabs legible; tst menuIcons-style legs updated for the new default; make check PASS.

Outcome: default flipped to `tabler` (s_defaultId; invalid/absent MainWindow/iconTheme resolves to tabler; native stays in the combo). Audit found 62 distinct chrome names vs 35 bundled — 26 imported into arora-tabler(+dark) from upstream tabler-icons v3.45.0 via TABLER_MAP, plus generated tor-onion (no upstream glyph) and tab-close; breeze(+dark) gained tab-close; legacy got a closetab.png alias. Dedupes: process-stop→player-stop, list-add→playlist-add, Close Tab→tab-close (browser-x) vs Close Window→window-close (x). names() now returns the union of all bundled sets. Verified: tst_aroraicon 14/14 (new defaultCoverage leg), tst_browsermainwindow 61/61 (new static-menu duplicate-glyph audit), tst_settingsdialog icon-theme legs 4/4, --icons-smoke PASS, grab set (menus/dark nav/tor/settings) reviewed clean. make check: check-warnings PASS + all suites green; browsermainwindow/schemehandlers flakes under the concurrent warnings build passed standalone (61/61, 25/25).

### TABS19

Firefox-sized tabs — user: 'make the tab size like in firefox.' The compact TABS07/08 band (~26-28px, maxThickness = max(fm,icon)+9 at tabbar.cpp:2175) reads thin next to Firefox's ~33px tab / ~36-38px strip. Spec: (a) HEIGHT — horizontal tab thickness lands at Firefox's normal-density metrics: ~33px tab face (~36px strip incl. strip padding) — retune the minThickness/maxThickness band so the Automatic (unpersisted) thickness equals it; a dragged-in RSZ02 strip height still overrides. Measure, don't guess: capture the real px the formula yields before/after on a standard DPI grab; vertical rail rows keep their own metrics (uniform row height per TABS18) but may adopt the same +pad if it doesn't break row uniformity. (b) WIDTH — Firefox tabs: ~76px min, ~225px max, every tab equal width while space allows (expanding squeeze), scroll arrows only once tabs hit the min — audit QTabBar expanding/usesScrollButtons/the 18-'M' extent cap (tabSizeHint ~2163): set a real minimumSizeHint ~76px so squeeze matches Firefox before scrolling kicks in; pinned tabs keep the TABS14 titled sizing — do NOT collapse pinned to favicon tiles. (c) THEM01-04's rounded cards must keep painting correctly at the taller band (corner radii, active-tab merge into the pane — grab to confirm). (d) group pills/container chips extents recheck at the new height. Verify: offscreen grabs at 1+many tabs show ~33px faces and equal-width squeeze matching Firefox behavior; strip grab shows the ~36px band; tst_tabbar/tabwidget sizing legs updated to the new constants; make check PASS.

### TRUST15

MOVED FROM MAIN LANE — user directive. Shield button gone from the nav bar — user screenshot on a LIVE WebEngine tab (hup.hu): bar reads [Arora][back][fwd][reload][home][reader][url-bar] with NO shield; separately it also hides on engine/widget tabs via setVisible(webView != nullptr) (siteshield.cpp ~91/117). Position target (user-stated): immediately RIGHT of the reader button — that IS the current slot ([..., reload, reader, SHIELD, splitter/urlbar], browsermainwindow.cpp ~2099) — keep it there. REPRODUCE FIRST — build a dump of m_navigationBar->actions() (id, action->isVisible(), widget->isVisible(), geometry) on a session-restored window with a real web tab, then fix whichever path leaves it absent. Candidates: (a) ctor-hidden widget + QToolBar::insertWidget — does a later setVisible(true) re-lay out? (b) applyNavBarOrder removeAction/addAction round-trip on the QWidgetAction — does the widget re-embed? (c) first bind racing session restore with no later currentChanged/tabsChanged pulse; (d) toolbar >> overflow; (e) action-level setVisible(false). THEN the non-web-tab part: shield stays VISIBLE on every tab — disabled + dimmed + honest tooltip on engine/widget tabs ('not available on this tab'), live rebind back on web tabs. Also verify security-high glyph renders in tabler + dark sets. Verify: fresh-launch + session-restored windows show the shield right of the reader button; servo/widget tabs show it disabled-with-reason; tst_browsermainwindow legs assert presence+position+state; make check PASS.

Outcome (2026-10-15): REPRODUCE — a scratch QToolBar probe pinned the bug class: a widget hidden in its ctor before addWidget() latches the generated QWidgetAction invisible, and widget-side setVisible(true) later never reaches the action's flag (the action governs the slot). SiteShieldButton did exactly that (setVisible(m_webView != nullptr) in ctor + setWebView), so any window whose first bind raced ahead of widget-state setup — session-restored windows, tabs adopted before locationBar::setWebView — could keep an action-hidden ghost; and on widget tabs the per-tab bar had no webview at all so the shield could never exist there. removeAction/addAction round-trips (applyNavBarOrder) re-embed correctly ONLY when the action itself is visible — so the fix is: never hide. FIX — this branch still had the TRUST13 per-LocationBar shield (TRUST14's nav-bar move is master-only); a LocationBar child can never satisfy 'visible on widget tabs' (widget tabs have a plain QLineEdit), so the shield moved to BrowserMainWindow::setupToolBar as one shared m_siteShield (parent m_navigationBar, objectName 'navSiteShield', QWidgetAction id 'siteShield' between 'reader' and 'locationBar' — on tor windows torCircuit still sits before it). setWebView(nullptr) now means disabled + 'Site privacy — not available on this tab', never hidden; bound tabs get enable + live icon/badge/tooltip as before. Rebind = m_siteShield->setWebView(currentTab()) on QTabWidget::currentChanged + TabWidget::tabsChanged (fires in makeNewTabOnProfile/addWidgetTab/closeTab/moveTabTo/adoptTransferredTab — session restore, tear-off out and in all covered) + an initial bind at setup. applyNavBarOrder round-trips the action fine (verified by the stored-order leg). LocationBar drops m_shield/include/addWidget; the padlock stays inside the field untouched (TRUST16's final shape already holds). security-high.svg exists in arora-tabler, arora-tabler-dark and legacy — dark-scheme preview leg re-bakes and re-checks the pixmap. Tests: tst_locationbar widgets() now asserts no shield child; contentBlockingShield exercises a standalone button (unbound disabled+tooltip, bind->enabled, popup anchor, panel, adblock toggle). tst_browsermainwindow siteShieldButton asserts navSiteShield presence, action+widget visibility, icon/pixmap non-null, dark flip, order siteShield=reader+1 & locationBar=siteShield+1, enabled on web tab, visible-disabled+reason on the Preferences widget tab, re-enable on switch back, and a stored-partial-order window (session-restore shape) keeps it visible+enabled. readerModeButton updated for the +1 slot. VERIFIED: tst_locationbar 39/39; tst_browsermainwindow 62/62 in the make check run (siteShieldButton + readerModeButton + navBarEditMode + navBarOrderPersistence all PASS); nav-bar action dump shows [appMenu][back][forward][stopReload][home][reader][siteShield][locationBar][sidebar][brand] all visible. make check: check-warnings PASS (zero warnings, src+tools), 66 programs — ONE unrelated leg failed: tst_ContainerManager::adblockAppliesOnContainerProfile (localhost adblock-interception timing, PASS standalone 3/3 — same class as the documented suite flakes). Caveat: an earlier interactive full-suite run DID hit the documented tor-latch cascade (slow tor startpage clobbers torTitleBarTitle's title leg -> s_torMode stays latched -> later legs run tor-window paths: splitter at 8 = [reader][torCircuit][siteShield][locationBar], no extensionBar/windowMenu, dead-SOCKS timeouts; teardown SIGSEGV) — identical class previously reproduced on clean HEAD, every affected leg green standalone (6/6 + tor sequence 11/11 rerun). Full autotest build clean with -j2.

Sets bound stroke=currentColor to #3d3d3d/#e8e8e8 like tabler; ISC + Feather-MIT LICENSE vendored per dir, THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES section 10 + README icon listings updated (also corrected the stale '35 icons each' wording — tabler/lucide ship 62). ### ICONS12

Remove the `native` icon set — user: 'remove the old default one, not the one which is the default now, but the original one.' The pre-ICONS10 default was `native` = QIcon::fromTheme on the OS theme — incomplete coverage caused the duplicate/generic-fallback glyphs the user originally complained about. Spec: (a) drop `native` from the selectable sets — Preferences > Appearance combo shows only bundled open-source sets (adwaita/breeze/tabler/legacy + lucide once ICONS11 lands, each with dark variant auto-pick); the combo's 'Native' row goes away; (b) BEHAVIOR — a stored iconTheme=native falls back to the current default (tabler) rather than erroring — existing profiles must not break; treat unknown ids the same; (c) CODE — s_nativeId and the native branch in AroraIcon (the get()/themeFor resolution + the names() entry) removed or retired; any code path that special-cased native (system-theme lookups, fallback ordering) simplified to bundled-only; (d) the underlying OS-theme FALLBACK inside Qt (a bundled glyph missing -> system lookup) — decide and document: keep as last-resort or refuse silently (recommend keep — a wrong-theme glyph beats a blank hole, and the coverage audits keep the miss set empty); (e) arora-legacy STAYS — it is a bundled set, not the thing the user means. ### TRUST17

User report: 'The shield popover not opened up correctly for the first time.' Same symptom class as the earlier Tor-circuit two-click bug (fixed already — see whatever pattern that fix used, likely lazy panel construction on first show / focus event eaten by createPopup). Reproduce first: click the shield on a live HTTPS tab once — expected: SitePanel popover opens positioned under the button; actual: nothing/mispositioned until second click. Audit candidates: (a) popover/panel lazily constructed inside the click handler — first click builds it but show()/positioning runs before polish/layout, second click works; (b) first show mispositions because the panel sizes itself after exec — anchor after adjustSize, not before; (c) WA_StyledBackground/stylesheet first-show flicker; (d) the TRUST15 rebind left the button's popup path unset until first WebView signal. Compare with how TorCircuitButton's popover does it now (that one's verified single-click) and mirror the pattern. Verify: fresh window → single click opens the panel correctly positioned on FIRST click, second click closes, repeat across tab switches/engine tabs-disabled state; tst leg simulating first click if feasible. make check PASS.

**Done:** Same InstantPopup stale-rect class as TOR19 — `QMenu::popup` reads `sizeHint()` before `aboutToShow`, and the hosted `SitePanel`'s layout-derived hint stayed stale across a closed-window content change (e.g. a permission row appearing), so the first click mapped the menu at the old, squashed size. Mirrored the Tor circuit fix: `SiteShieldButton` stores the panel's `QWidgetAction` (`m_panelAction`), `aboutToShow` → `populateMenu()` refreshes then toggles the action's visibility to force Qt to re-measure, and a new `SitePanel::refreshed()` signal feeds `growMenuToFit()` so an already-open popup grows to content (never shrinks — same as Tor). The closed panel stays synced via `WebPermissionManager::changed`/`AdBlockManager::rulesChanged` → `refresh()`. Two real bugs surfaced under it: the panel's own `sizeHint()` still read stale inside `aboutToShow` because `rebuildPermissionRows()` never propagated geometry — fixed with `updateGeometry()` at the mutation site; and that method's `delete item->widget()` left every nested row layout's children orphaned on the box (leaked Revoke buttons painting over the popup — plausibly the user's actual symptom), now drains child items properly. New `tst_locationbar::shieldPopupFirstShow` serves a real-origin page via QTcpServer, writes a Notifications permission out-of-band to force divergence, calls `menu->sizeHint()` before `popup()` to reproduce the pre-measure, and verifies first-show geometry matches the live hint plus grow/shrink across add/remove. Pre-fix repro confirmed: popup opened at 395px vs required 403. Verified: tst_locationbar 40/40, tst_browsermainwindow 62/62, tst_webpermissions 22/22, check-warnings PASS, make check 1 unrelated pre-existing failure (tabwidget verticalTabDefaultWidth 35 vs 33 — fails identically on the unpatched tree) offscreen --disable-gpu.

### TABS20

User screenshot: tab favicon sits visibly ABOVE/OUTSIDE the tab face — glyph's top edge sticks past the tab's top border (looks clipped). Likely a TABS19 fallout: the tab face grew to ~33px but the icon's vertical centering didn't follow — audit the TabBar paint path: is the icon rect computed with hardcoded y-offset from the old 26-28px face, or is iconRect centered on the old band height instead of the new face rect? Check both horizontal-mode face paint AND dragged-strip height (the dragged-height override may use a different centering path — user's screenshot may be a dragged-height strip). Also verify against the THEM02 rounded-card margins — if the face is drawn inset but the icon is centered on the full rect, the icon clears the card's visual top. Fix so the icon is optically centered inside the PAINTED face (the card), not the layout rect. Check title baseline too while there. Verify: grab a horizontal strip — favicon vertically centered inside the card at default AND dragged heights; tst leg asserts icon rect within face rect + symmetric margins; vertical rail unaffected. make check PASS.

**Done:** Root cause found in the CONT06 two-level strip rather than TABS19 metrics — the favicon is a real widget installed via `setTabButton` on `freeSide()`, positioned by Qt through `SE_TabBarTabLeftButton`/`RightButton` in *strip* space, so no painter-side change could ever reach it. The old `shiftChildrenBelowStrip()` only ran from `tabLayoutChange()` and applied an incremental `y += band` guarded by `y() < band`; Qt's `layoutTab()`/`layoutWidgets()` paths (invoked by `setCurrentIndex()`, move drags, scroll reflows) re-place the buttons without that hook, parking the favicon back inside the 23px header band — reproduced as face `QRect(0,23 225x33)` vs favicon `QRect(12,10 16x16)`, and on dragged heights a legitimate strip-space `y >= band` read as "already shifted". The function now re-derives each per-tab button's style sub-element rect via `initStyleOption` + `subElementRect` and pins `y = rect.top() + band` while keeping the x Qt chose (drag offsets intact); the preview/'+'/rename editor keep their own band-aware placement, vertical-row buttons stay on `layoutRowTabButtons()`, and non-tab strip children (scroll buttons) keep the once-per-placement shift. Re-run sites: `paintEvent()` before the band paints, plus the `currentChanged` handler so geometry is right before the next repaint. Verified: new `tst_TabWidget::tabButtonBandAnchor` asserts containment in `tabRect.translated(0, band)` and ≤4px top/bottom margin asymmetry across initial/tab-switch/dragged-56/restore-auto legs with a `band-anchor.png` grab; the test fails on the unpatched tree with the reported geometry. tst_tabwidget 26/26, tst_tabbar 17/17, tst_containermanager 34/34, make check rc=0 (check-warnings PASS, 68 programs 0 failed) offscreen --disable-gpu.

### DOWN05

User report: 'On the Downloads sidebar the clean entire list (remove all) button is not there at the top.' The buildDownloadsPage header currently holds only the section title + close button — no clear-list control. Add a clear-all affordance to the downloads header row (left of the close button, icon-button style matching sidebarCloseButton — use the AroraIcon trash/clear glyph, accessible name + tooltip 'Clear download list'). Semantics: removes completed/failed/cancelled entries via DownloadManager::cleanup() or the model's clear path — it must NEVER delete the downloaded FILES and must NOT remove in-progress downloads (only finished states); if a removal could kill an active download, exclude it. If the action is destructive to list history, a lightweight confirm (or undo toast if a pattern exists) is fine — check what History sidebar's Clear does for the affordance pattern. Button disables itself honestly when the list is empty or only active downloads remain. Verify: sidebar grab — clear button visible in header; click with mixed entries → finished ones gone, active ones untouched, files on disk untouched; empty list → disabled. tst leg covering the model clear. make check PASS.

**Done:** `sidebarClearDownloads` — an autoRaise `QToolButton` with the house `edit-clear` chrome glyph, accessible name + tooltip "Clear download list" — sits in the downloads header immediately left of the close X. Clicking raises the same `QMessageBox::question` Yes/No confirm the History menu's Clear uses (`HistoryMenu::clearHistoryDialog` is the house pattern), then calls `DownloadManager::cleanup()` → `DownloadModel::removeRows`, which only drops rows that are `downloadedSuccessfully()` or offer Try Again — in-flight transfers are structurally unremovable and completed files are never touched (a failed/cancelled row sheds only its half-written partial, same as the list's Del key and SEC01). Enabled state is honest: new `DownloadManager::hasRemovableItems()` mirrors the removeRows predicate exactly, and the button re-evaluates it on the model's `rowsInserted`/`rowsRemoved`/`modelReset`/`dataChanged` (the last carries every finish/fail transition via `updateRow`), so it reads disabled on an empty list, enabled once a row is clearable, and disabled again when only active downloads remain. Verified: new `tst_BrowserMainWindow::downloadsSidebarClearList` — header order (clear left of X), disabled on empty, enabled after a finished download, in-flight second row keeps it enabled, "No" keeps both rows, "Yes" drops only the finished row with the in-flight item and the completed file on disk untouched, disabled while only the active row remains, then stop→enabled→Yes empties the list; `ARORA_SIDEBAR_GRAB_DIR` grab (downloads-clear.png) reviewed — button visible enabled in the header over a mixed list. tst_browsermainwindow 63/63, tst_downloadmanager 42/42, make check: check-warnings PASS + all programs green except the known unrelated tormanager `circuitMenuNoRequeryLoop` flake (PASS standalone) offscreen --disable-gpu.

### MENU17

User: 'make the logo bigger but keep the ratio — let's say 25%.' After MENU16 the app-menu button inherits the nav bar's 18px iconSize. Bump JUST the app-menu button to ~22-23px (18 * 1.25 = 22.5 — round to the value that renders cleanly, e.g. 22 or 24; prefer the SVG-native size that avoids scaling blur if the glyph has one). Constraints: (a) only the app-menu button — siblings stay 18px, this is a deliberate brand-button accent like Firefox's app menu; (b) 'keep the ratio' = scale the glyph uniformly, never stretch — the SVG scales anyway but verify no fixed W/H override distorts; (c) keep the button square/compact — grow the icon, not gratuitous padding; check the button doesn't grow the nav bar's row height beyond what it is now (bar height should stay driven by the URL field); (d) private/Tor window variants must follow (torAppMenuIcon path too). Verify: grab the nav bar — logo glyph visibly ~25% larger than sibling buttons but the bar height and sibling sizes unchanged; tst asserts the button's iconSize ≈ 1.25× bar iconSize. make check PASS.

**Done:** The catch: a bare `setIconSize(23)` on a toolbar-hosted button changes only the accessor — `QToolButton::initStyleOption` unconditionally rewrites `opt.iconSize` to the parent `QToolBar`'s (18) at paint AND sizeHint time, so the property read 23 while the pixels stayed 18. The new file-local `AppMenuButton` subclass (next to `AroraLogoEngine`) restores `iconSize()` on the style option at the two read sites — `paintEvent` (verbatim Qt body + the restore before `drawComplexControl`, which also fixes the tor stylesheet path) and `sizeHint` (QToolButton's computation replicated with the corrected option). Constraint (c) needed a second fix: the first cut let the face grow to 31x30 and pushed the nav row 34px -> 38px (measured against the MENU16 grab baseline) — `sizeHint` now caps the face at a square `QToolButton::sizeHint().height() + 1` (26x26), which is what the URL field already demands, so the bar is back to 34px with the 23px mark filling the cell; `boundedTo` in `CE_ToolButtonLabel` means a snug face can only shrink the glyph a pixel on fat-frame styles, never clip or stretch it. Icon size derives from the live bar size (`qRound(iconSize * 1.25)` = 23), square request keeps the ratio, and the private/tor variants follow automatically since the size lives on the shared button (the `torAppMenuIcon` disc swap is orthogonal). Verified: `tst_BrowserMainWindow::appMenuButton` extended — square cell, `|iconSize.h - bar*1.25| <= 1`, the Back sibling still at 18, face `> sibling` but `<= sibling + 1`; private + tor legs carry the same 1.25x asserts. ### TRUST19

User report: padlock shows YELLOW on a trustable HTTPS page — user CLARIFIED: 'On normal tab the coloring is ok, for the lock.' So the yellow sighting was on the TOR window (or otherwise non-normal context), and normal-tab tiers work. Revised question: should a Tor window show green when TLS itself verifies? The threat-probe is honestly neutral on Tor (no site-probe feeds), but TLS validity IS known — the exit-to-site cert chain is still verified by the browser (TlsVerifier log lines fire on Tor tabs). Decide honestly: if the verifier's verdict on Tor carries enough to say 'connection is valid HTTPS', GREEN is justified — the lock's job is connection security, not site reputation (that's the shield's surface). Yellow should then mean 'TLS verdict genuinely unknown/unverifiable' — e.g. no verifier answer, non-HTTPS, or a degraded state. If instead the verifier never actually runs on the Tor profile, yellow is honest — verify which it is before deciding. Audit the tier computation in SitePadlockButton refresh + what feeds it (TlsVerifier verdict, SiteDecisionStore): (a) if 'trusted' requires an explicit stored SiteDecision, no site will ever be green — the baseline must be: valid verified TLS + no flags = GREEN; stored negative decisions / warnings → red; only genuinely no-information states (Tor no-probe, failed/empty verdict) → yellow; (b) check the TlsVerifier verdict enum mapping — live log shows 'TlsVerifier: host:443 2' — if 2 is a neutral/unknown verdict returned even for valid certs, the verifier-to-tier mapping is broken; (c) timing — padlock may bind/refresh before the TLS verdict lands and never re-refresh on verdict-arrival signal. Fix so the lock's color means what TLS actually knows: valid cert → green in EVERY window type (normal, private, Tor — the cert chain verifies the same); yellow only when the verdict is genuinely absent/unknown; red for warnings/threats. If audit shows Tor tabs never get a verdict at all, either wire the verifier there or keep yellow + document why. Verify: fixture/assert — valid HTTPS → green; documented no-probe path → yellow; warning state → red; refresh fires on verdict arrival not just navigation. make check PASS.

Blocked (2026-10-15): the entire fix target is absent from ui-work. `src/locationbar/sitepadlock.{h,cpp}`, `src/network/tlsverifier.{h,cpp}`, `src/sitedecisionstore.{h,cpp}` (+ rustcore sitedecisions.rs) exist only on master — commit 8182b3f3 (TRUST14) is not an ancestor of ui-work (`git merge-base --is-ancestor` checked); grep finds no padlock/TlsVerifier/SiteDecisionStore symbol anywhere under this worktree's src/. The in-field surface here is LocationBarSiteIcon and the nav-bar SiteShieldButton is content-blocking only — no trust tier exists to fix. Lane rules forbid editing the main-lane worktree (where the code lives at /home/szefi/Documents/arora) and forbid merging master into ui-work; porting the security-lane machinery here would duplicate it and conflict at the next ui-work->master merge. Action needed: either merge master into ui-work (loop operator / next MERGEWATCH), or re-queue this row on the main lane's queue where the code is present.

### TRUST20

Follow-up to TRUST18. TWO user reports, same class: (1) 'The reader button on tor changes back to black when rolling on the page' — glyph correct at window open, reverts to black on a state change during scroll (reader probe verdict flips enabled/disabled as content streams in); (2) 'At the TOR Windows the sidebar's icons are black so not able to see them' — the sidebar icon strip (bookmarks/history/downloads/notes/etc) paints unthemed glyphs on the dark Tor chrome ALWAYS, not just on state change. Both = the themed-icon resolution never reaching these widgets. Sidebar icons may be set once at SidebarPanel construction from the ambient (light) icon set — audit every icon-bearing widget in the Tor window chrome, not just the nav bar. — the glyph is correct (theme-aware) at window open, but reverts to black on a state change during scroll — almost certainly when ReaderMode's probe verdict flips the button enabled/disabled (probe verdicts fire as content streams in during scroll). So TRUST18 fixed the INITIAL icon but the refresh-on-state-change path still paints the unthemed variant. Audit: find every place the reader button repaints its icon (setEnabled side-effects, explicit setIcon on verdict change, toolButton auto-repaint with a cached unthemed QIcon) — the themed glyph must be applied in ALL icon-setting paths for the button, or better: resolve the themed icon once per theme-change and reuse it, so no state transition can paint a stale variant. Check whether the same staleness bites OTHER buttons whose icons refresh on state (engine indicator on swap, reload→stop transition, padlock on verdict) — if the refresh path is shared, fix it once at the source rather than per-button. Also audit: extension action icons and any other dynamically-set glyphs in the window. ### MENU19

User confirmed on the post-MENU17 build: enlarge the corner app-menu button logo ANOTHER ~25% — current is bar-iconSize × 1.25 (~23px at the bar's 18px) → target ~29px (bar × ~1.6, or 23 × 1.25 ≈ 29). Keep uniform scaling — no distortion, SVG scales cleanly. Same constraints as MENU17: only this button grows, siblings stay 18px, the nav bar's row height must NOT grow (URL field drives it — check the button's sizeHint doesn't push the bar taller; if it does, cap height not icon), hit-rect stays at least as big as the glyph, private/Tor variants follow. If 29px measurably crowds the bar, 28px is acceptable — the spec is 'visibly bigger by about a quarter vs current', not a pixel religion. Verify: grab the bar — glyph clearly ~25% larger than current build's, bar height unchanged, siblings unchanged; update the MENU17 test assert (it pins ~1.25× — retarget to the new ratio). make check PASS.

**Done:** iconSize is now `qRound(bar * 1.6)` = 29px. The catch: `CE_ToolButton` bounds the pixmap request to `widget.rect`, and `QToolBarLayout` stretches every item to (bar height - layout margins) — so a 29px mark needs a ~30px widget, which with the style's 4px margins (PM_ToolBarItemMargin + PM_ToolBarFrameWidth) would push the row 34 -> 38. The face can't stay 26 (glyph would paint 26 = +13%, not +25%) and can't paint past the widget (backing-store clip). Fix: `AppMenuButton::sizeHint` retargets the cap to a square at `iconSize+1` (30x30) with an `expandedTo(iconSize)` floor for thin styles, and a new file-local `NavBarToolBar` subclass compacts the toolbar layout's vertical margins to <=2px — the layout (not the widget margins, which stack) is the row's padding source, verified empirically: 30px face + 2+2 margins = 34px bar, location field still 26 centered. `QToolBar::changeEvent` on StyleChange calls `updateMarginAndSpacing` which rebuilds margins+spacing from style metrics — real triggers exist (tor bar setStyleSheet, theme-rewrap `QApplication::setStyle`, app chrome sheet) — so `NavBarToolBar::changeEvent` re-asserts the compact margins + authored spacing right after the base reset; verified live by the tor grab landing at 34px post-stylesheet. Verified: `tst_browsermainwindow` 65/65 — appMenuButton retargeted (|iconSize.h - bar*1.6| <= 1, face `<= iconSize+1`, bar `<= field minHeight + 8`, square cell, hit-rect >= glyph), private + tor legs carry the 1.6x asserts, chromeMetrics' margin contract moved to row-vs-field height. ARORA_MENU_GRAB_DIR: `app-menu-navbar{,-tor}.png` 533/992 x 34 (baseline-identical), `app-menu-button{,-tor}.png` 30x30 with the ~29px mark filling the face — clearly ~25% over the MENU17 build's 23px; siblings still 18px.

### MEM05  (moved from main-lane MEM04 — sliced: diagnosis only)

User report: starting from telex.hu, opening an article and going back repeatedly — the memory indicator always rises, never falls. THIS TASK IS MEASURE-ONLY: no fixes. Deliverables: (a) establish WHERE the indicator's number comes from — /proc RSS vs a QtWebEngine-internal figure vs our own tally; an honest RSS that climbs is a different problem than a lying counter; (b) build a deterministic ~20-cycle article→back fixture (local pages fine — a link chain + back nav, no live site needed), snapshot RSS + any heap figure per cycle, emit a per-cycle table; (c) verdict: does the slope plateau (bounded cache) or climb linearly (real leak)? — extend the loop to ~40 cycles if 20 is ambiguous; (d) IF a leak is indicated, run per-cycle allocation accounting (QUAL02's methodology) to name the top growing allocation site among the suspects — TlsVerifier records, PrivacyRequestInterceptor state, site-decision cache, history/completer entries, ReaderMode probe artifacts, bf-stack WebContents refs, the PERF01 memoizing evaluate wrapper, schedulePageScripts re-arming (prior audit: scripts replaced by name, ruled out) — naming the site is in scope, fixing it is NOT (MEM06). NORMAL WINDOW ONLY — other surfaces are MEM07. Verify: the fixture runs deterministically, the per-cycle table + plateau/leak verdict + named allocation site (if leak) are recorded in the task Notes. make check PASS.

**MEM05 results (2026-10-15):**

- (a) Indicator source: `MemIndicator::refresh` → `Engine::Page::residentMemoryKb()` → `processMemoryKb(renderProcessId())` → `/proc/<renderer-pid>/status` VmRSS, 2s poll (statusbarwidgets.cpp ~321, engineinterface.cpp ~28). It is an HONEST real-RSS readout, not a counter — and the RSS genuinely climbs.
- (b) Fixture: `autotests/memcycle` — QTcpServer loopback serving index + per-cycle-unique ~130KB articles + shared css/js/svg; index→article→back loop, per-cycle samples of renderer VmRSS + renderer RssAnon + app VmRSS (`ARORA_MEM_CYCLES/SETTLE_MS/REPORT/SAMEURL` knobs; per-cycle table via `ARORA_MEM_REPORT`).
- (c) Verdict: LINEAR CLIMB, no plateau — normal window renderer +5392 kB/cycle over 20 cycles (114→216MB), still +5429 kB/cycle at 40 cycles (→326MB). RssAnon tracks VmRSS ~1:1 → anonymous heap, not file caches. App-process series flat (+90-180 kB/cycle noise).
- (d) Named site: NOT an Arora object. Bare-`QWebEngineView` control (no WebPage, interceptors, channel bootstrap, ReaderMode, adblock/autofill, HistoryManager) climbs identically at +5773 kB/cycle → upstream Chromium per-navigation retention (~5.4-5.8MB per committed document, session-history/frame-tree). Corroborating: `history()->clear()` post-loop released 0 kB; `ARORA_MEM_SAMEURL=1` (same article every cycle) climbs identically → per-NAV not per-unique-URL. All Arora-side suspects eliminated: TlsVerifier/interceptor/site-decision/history entries live in the flat app series; PERF01 verdict cache bounded (4096, app-side); schedulePageScripts replace-by-name confirmed (adblockpage.cpp:599, autofillmanager.cpp:734); ReaderMode probe is transient app-side.
- Slope table (renderer kB/cycle / app kB/cycle, 20 cycles): normal 5392/98, private 5202/177, background 8240/2, second-window 4238/24, engine-tab(fake) 0/0, bare-engine 5773/52. MEM07 owns the tor leg (needs faketor SOCKS plumbing) + Servo.
- Fixture mechanics note: the index gate must wait on `historyCount()>=1` not `url()==index` — `WebView::url()` returns `m_initialUrl` instantly and a loadUrl against an uncommitted load REPLACES the history entry (canGoBack never true).
- MEM06 guidance: the lever is upstream (cap/flush session-history document retention, e.g. limiting retained entries or recycling renderers), not freeing an Arora object. `ARORA_MEM_LEAK_GATE=1` arms the 1.5MB/cycle renderer assertion for the post-fix comparison; app-series gate (1MB/cycle) is always on.
- Verify: tst_memcycle 8/8 (6 surface legs + init/cleanup) deterministic offscreen --disable-gpu; make check rc=0 — all gates PASS (warnings/supplychain/engine-audit/rustcoverage) + full suite 0 failed; two interrupted-run test edits were repaired first (browsermainwindow darkThemeReaderGlyph sampled the Accent "suggested" glyph post-availability — baseline legs moved ahead of the article load; webview linkOpenRefererDiscipline's spy attached post-commit — gate now also accepts an already-finished committed load).

### MEM06

Memory climb fix — applies MEM05's verdict. needs:task:MEM05. MEM05 measured: renderer climbs ~+5.4MB/cycle LINEARLY, no plateau at 40 cycles; a BARE QWebEngineView control (no Arora code) climbs identically → upstream Chromium per-navigation document retention, NOT an Arora object. All Arora-side suspects eliminated (see MEM05 evidence). The fixture exists: `autotests/memcycle` with ARORA_MEM_CYCLES/SETTLE_MS/REPORT/SAMEURL knobs and ARORA_MEM_LEAK_GATE=1 arming a 1.5MB/cycle renderer assertion.

Experiment legs, IN ORDER — test the mechanism before committing to a fix:
(a) BFCache A/B — rerun the fixture with QTWEBENGINE_CHROMIUM_FLAGS='--disable-features=BackForwardCache'. If the slope dies → confirmed BFCache frozen-page retention; the fix is a chromium-flag toggle behind a Settings option (honest label: 'Keep pages in memory for instant Back — uses more memory', default ON) since QtWebEngine has no bf-cache API and the flag is startup-only. Requires restart for toggling — say so in the UI.
(b) Memory-pressure leg — if (a) still climbs, the retention may be Chromium's discardable/purgeable pool that only releases under pressure: fire a memory-pressure notification (Chromium flag or WebEngine memory-pressure API if exposed) mid-loop and re-sample. If it drops → soft retention, not a true leak: document the plateau-under-pressure behaviour, optionally wire a periodic low-priority purge, do NOT ship a 'fix' for non-leaking memory.
(c) lifecycleState — QWebEnginePage::setLifecycleState(Discarded) frees renderer resources; the background-tab leg had the WORST slope (8240 kB/cycle). If (a)/(b) don't kill it, apply lifecycle discarding to non-visible tabs (ties into existing SleepingTabs machinery if present) and re-measure the background leg.
(d) Last resort — recreate WebContents past N navigations; ugly (loses scroll/form state), only if a real unbounded leak survives a-c.
Verify: memcycle slope before vs after, gate legs pass, chosen mechanism's tradeoff is honestly surfaced in Settings. make check PASS.

**MEM06 results (2026-10-16):**

- (a) BFCache A/B: `QTWEBENGINE_CHROMIUM_FLAGS='--disable-gpu --disable-features=BackForwardCache'` on normalTabCycle → renderer slope **5389 kB/cycle vs 5392 baseline** — NO change. Retention is not BFCache frozen pages. (QtWebEngine does not drive BFCache anyway — history()->clear() already freed 0 kB in MEM05.)
- (b) Pressure leg: `ARORA_MEM_GC=1` (new fixture knob — `runJavaScript("gc()")` under `--js-flags=--expose-gc` after each back nav, the reachable proxy for a memory-pressure purge on the unified v8+oilpan heap) → renderer slope **5392 → 150 kB/cycle** (RSS 216→118 MB). **Verdict: soft retention, not a leak** — the bytes are collectable garbage; desktop Linux just never sends the renderer a memory-pressure signal, so V8 lazy-GC never runs.
- (c) lifecycleState: Qt's public lifecycle API is the wireable purge — freezing a HIDDEN page routes Chromium's CRITICAL memory-pressure notification through its renderer (no `--expose-gc`, no debug surface). Fixture leg `ARORA_MEM_FREEZE=1` on backgroundTabCycle: **8240 → 59 kB/cycle**. Frozen is illegal on a visible page, so the shipped sweep only reaches background tabs — foreground retention stays reclaimable-on-real-pressure (spec-sanctioned: no fake fix; MEM08 owns the manual flush).
- Shipped: `TabWidget::purgeInactiveTabs()` — 2-min QTimer sweep (gated by new `tabs/memoryPurge`, default ON, live-applied via loadSettings) freeze-pulses each eligible background page Active→Frozen→~600ms→Active. Exemptions: current tab, non-Active lifecycle (sleeping/discarded), loading, recentlyAudible, active download — form-dirty and pinned intentionally NOT exempt (freeze keeps all page state, no reload). Restore is QPointer+state-guarded so a tab discarded mid-pulse is never revived. New Preferences → Performance → Memory Saver checkbox "Reclaim memory from inactive tabs every few minutes" (tooltip states the pause tradeoff). MemIndicator tooltip now honestly notes the figure is memory the engine reclaims when the system needs it.
- Fixture: `ARORA_MEM_GC` + `ARORA_MEM_FREEZE` knobs added; the FREEZE leg drives the shipped `purgeInactiveTabs()` end-to-end (background leg is the one that flattens — cycled current tabs are correctly skipped).
- Verified: `ARORA_MEM_LEAK_GATE=1 ARORA_MEM_FREEZE=1 ./tst_memcycle backgroundTabCycle` → PASS at 61 kB/cycle (gate 1536); `tst_tabwidget::memoryPurge` (Frozen→Active pulse observed, current/empty tabs skipped, discarded tab never revived); `tst_settingsdialog::performancePage` +4 legs (default-on, persist round-trip, reset). make check rc=0 — check-warnings PASS, supplychain/engine-audit/rustcoverage PASS, 83 programs 0 failed, offscreen --disable-gpu.
- Foreground-tab story for MEM07/MEM08: normal/private/second-window legs still climb ~5.4 MB/cycle by design (page is current → sweep skips it). It is soft retention — do NOT chase it in MEM07 as a leak; MEM08's discard+reload flush is the user-facing reclaim.

Tor leg mechanics (all in tst_memcycle torTabCycle → re-exec'd child, `ARORA_MEMCYCLE_TOR_CHILD`):
- faketor.py gained opt-in `FAKETOR_SOCKS_RELAY=1`: a real SOCKS5 CONNECT relay restricted to loopback + own-interface addresses (socket.if_nameindex/SIOCGIFADDR enumeration — strictly on-box), logging every target to `<DataDir>/socks-connect-log`. Default refuse-everything behavior unchanged for existing tor tests.
- The leg re-execs the test binary so tor mode + fail-closed proxy exist before BrowserApplication's ctor latches Chromium's proxy config.

Qt quirk found: QTcpServer::listen() itself goes through the application SOCKS proxy (BIND) — the child clears the proxy for the bind only.

Both engines now exempt `is_private_or_local_host` targets (src/engine/navigationpolicy.cpp C++ fallback + src/rustcore/src/policy.rs), mirroring the https-first path; tst_navigationpolicy gained the loopback-exemption legs.
